\int-\delta\bar{F}_{n}[g_{t}](\theta)\cdot h(\theta)\geq\varepsilon>0

for all
t\in[0,T]
, where
\varepsilon<\min(1/2,1/(4C_{0}),1/(16\alpha))
.
Fix
L
such that

C_{0}e^{-c_{0}L^{2}}=\varepsilon/4
, i.e.
L=\sqrt{(-1/c_{0})\log[\varepsilon/(4C_{0})]}
, and denote

K=C_{0}e^{L}\sqrt{p},\qquad A(t)={-}D_{\mathrm{KL}}(h\|g_{t}),\qquad B(t)=-F_{n}(q_{t},g_{t}).

Then
H_{t}(L)=K\sqrt{B^{\prime}(t)}+\varepsilon/4
.
Then the above implies that for each
t\in[0,T]
,

\text{either }K\sqrt{B^{\prime}(t)}\geq 1/2-\varepsilon/4\geq 1/4\qquad\text{ or }\qquad\varepsilon\leq 2K\sqrt{B^{\prime}(t)}+\varepsilon/2+2A^{\prime}(t).

In the latter case
\frac{\varepsilon}{4}\leq K\sqrt{B^{\prime}(t)}+A^{\prime}(t)
, so

\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{8}\leq\frac{\varepsilon}{2}A^{\prime}(t)+\frac{\varepsilon}{2}K\sqrt{B^{\prime}(t)}\leq\frac{\varepsilon}{2}A^{\prime}(t)+\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{16}+K^{2}B^{\prime}(t)
, the latter inequality applying
Cauchy-Schwarz. This implies

\displaystyle K^{2}B^{\prime}(t)+A^{\prime}(t)\cdot\varepsilon/2\geq\varepsilon^{2}/8.

This holds also in the former case because

K^{2}B^{\prime}(t)+A^{\prime}(t)\varepsilon/2\geq 1/16+A^{\prime}(t)\varepsilon/2\geq\varepsilon^{2}/8
,
the latter inequality using
\varepsilon<\min(1/2,1/(16\alpha))
and

\displaystyle A^{\prime}(t)=\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\int_{-M}^{M}h(\theta)\log g_{t}(\theta)=\alpha\cdot\int_{-M}^{M}\Big(\Big[\mathcal{N}_{\tau}\ast\frac{\bar{q}_{t}}{\mathcal{N}_{\tau}\ast g_{t}}\Big](\theta)-1\Big)h(\theta)\geq\alpha\cdot\int_{-M}^{M}{-}h(\theta)=-\alpha.

Then, recalling the definitions of
A(t),B(t)
and
integrating from time
t
to time
T
gives

K^{2}\left(F_{n}(q_{t},g_{t})-F_{n}(q_{T},g_{T})\right)+\frac{\varepsilon}{2}\left(D_{\mathrm{KL}}(h\|g_{t})-D_{\mathrm{KL}}(h\|g_{T})\right)\geq\frac{(T-t)\varepsilon^{2}}{8}.

We have
\limsup_{t\to 0}F_{n}(q_{t},g_{t})\leq F_{n}(q_{0},g_{0})
by Lemma

B.4
, and
\lim_{t\to 0}D_{\mathrm{KL}}(h\|g_{t})=D_{\mathrm{KL}}(h\|g_{0})
.
Then

K^{2}(F_{n}(q_{0},g_{0})-F_{n,*})+\frac{\varepsilon}{2}D_{\mathrm{KL}}(h\|g_{0})\geq\frac{T\varepsilon^{2}}{8}.

This is a contradiction for

\displaystyle T

\displaystyle\geq C\bigg[\frac{p\cdot e^{C\sqrt{{-}\log\varepsilon}}}{\varepsilon^{2}}(F_{n}(q_{0},g_{0})-F_{n,*})+\frac{1}{\varepsilon}D_{\mathrm{KL}}(h\|g_{0})\bigg]

and some large enough constant
C:=C(M,\tau)>0
,
which proves (
3.14
).

For the second claim (
3.15
), since
\bar{F}_{n}(g_{t})\leq F_{n}(q_{t},g_{t})\leq F_{n}(q_{0},g_{0})
where the second inequality follows from
(
B.19
), we have that
g_{t}

belongs to
\mathcal{K}
for all
t\geq 0
. Let
t\leq T
be as in (
3.14
) such that
\int_{-M}^{M}-\delta\bar{F}_{n}[g_{t}](\theta)h(\theta)\leq\varepsilon
, and define

H(\lambda)=\bar{F}_{n}(\lambda h+(1-\lambda)g_{t})
. Since also
h\in\mathcal{K}
, by the assumed convexity of
\bar{F}_{n}
on
\mathcal{K}

we have
H^{\prime\prime}(\lambda)\geq 0
for all
\lambda\in[0,1]

so
H(1)\geq H(0)+H^{\prime}(0)
, which is equivalent to

\bar{F}_{n}(g_{t})-\bar{F}_{n}(h)\leq\int_{-M}^{M}-\delta\bar{F}_{n}[g_{t}](\theta)h(\theta)\mathrm{d}\theta\leq\varepsilon,

as desired.
∎

B.4
Proof of (
3.17
)

We verify (
3.17
) under Assumption
A

and the condition
\tau^{2}<\sigma^{2}/\|\mathbf{X}\|_{\operatorname{op}}^{2}-\delta
.
Recall that
F_{n,*}=\min_{g\in\mathcal{P}(M)}{-}\frac{1}{p}\log P_{g}(\mathbf{y})
.
From the form (
1.4
), we have

P_{g}(\mathbf{y})\leq(2\pi\sigma^{2})^{-n/2}
, hence

F_{n,*}\geq\frac{n}{2p}\log(2\pi\sigma^{2})
.
Next, recall from (
2.11
) that we have

\displaystyle F_{n}(q_{0},g_{0})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2p}\mathbb{E}_{{\bm{\varphi}}\sim q_{0}}[(\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{X}{\bm{\varphi}})^{\top}{\bm{\Sigma}}^{-1}(\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{X}{\bm{\varphi}})]+\frac{1}{p}D_{\mathrm{KL}}(q_{0}\|(\mathcal{N}_{\tau}*g_{0})^{\otimes p})

\displaystyle\hskip 72.26999pt+\frac{n}{2p}\log(2\pi)+\frac{1}{2p}\log\det({\bm{\Sigma}}).

(B.21)

Note that

\mathbb{E}_{{\bm{\varphi}}\sim q_{0}}[(\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{X}{\bm{\varphi}})^{\top}{\bm{\Sigma}}^{-1}(\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{X}{\bm{\varphi}})]\leq\|{\bm{\Sigma}}^{-1}\|_{{\operatorname{op}}}\mathbb{E}_{{\bm{\varphi}}\sim q_{0}}[\|\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{X}{\bm{\varphi}}\|_{2}^{2}]\leq 2\|{\bm{\Sigma}}^{-1}\|_{{\operatorname{op}}}(\|\mathbf{y}\|_{2}^{2}+\mathbb{E}_{q_{0}}[\|{\bm{\varphi}}\|_{2}^{2}]\|\mathbf{X}\|_{\operatorname{op}}^{2}),

where
\|{\bm{\Sigma}}^{-1}\|_{{\operatorname{op}}}=(\sigma^{2}-\tau^{2}\|\mathbf{X}\|_{{\operatorname{op}}}^{2})^{-1}<1/(\delta\|\mathbf{X}\|_{\operatorname{op}}^{2})\leq 1/(C_{*}^{2}\delta)

and
\|\mathbf{X}\|_{\operatorname{op}}^{2}\leq C_{*}^{2}
by Assumption
A
.
Furthermore, in view of (
1.2
),
\|\mathbf{y}\|_{2}^{2}\leq 2(\|\mathbf{X}\|_{{\operatorname{op}}}^{2}\|{\bm{\theta}}\|_{2}^{2}+\|{\bm{\varepsilon}}\|_{2}^{2})
, where

\|\mathbf{X}\|_{{\operatorname{op}}}^{2}\|{\bm{\theta}}\|_{2}^{2}\leq C_{*}^{2}M^{2}p
with probability one, and

\|{\bm{\varepsilon}}\|_{2}^{2}\leq 2\sigma^{2}n
with probability approaching 1 as
n\to\infty
.
Thus the first term in (
B.21
) is at most

C(1+\frac{n+\mathbb{E}_{q_{0}}[\|{\bm{\varphi}}\|_{2}^{2}]}{p})
for some

C=C(\sigma,\delta,M,C_{*})
. For the last term,
we have
\log\det({\bm{\Sigma}})\leq n\log\lVert{\bm{\Sigma}}\rVert_{{\operatorname{op}}}\leq n\log\sigma^{2}
, and combining these statements
proves the desired (
3.17
).

B.5
Proof of Proposition
3.8

We need two lemmas before proving Proposition
3.8
. The first lemma computes the Hessian of
\bar{F}_{n}
.

Lemma B.5
.

Let
g\in\mathcal{P}(M)
and
\chi\in\Gamma
be such that
g_{t}\equiv g+t\chi\in\mathcal{P}(M)
for
t\in[0,1]
. Then

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}^{2}}{\mathrm{d}t^{2}}\bar{F}_{n}(g_{t})=\bar{H}_{n}[g_{t}](\chi)

and

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}^{2}}{\mathrm{d}t^{2}}\mathbb{E}_{g}\bar{F}_{n}(g_{t})\Big|_{t=0}=\frac{1}{p}\int\frac{(\sum_{j=1}^{p}\int P(\mathbf{y}|{\bm{\varphi}})\prod_{k:k\neq j}\mathcal{N}_{\tau}\ast g(\varphi_{k})\cdot\mathcal{N}_{\tau}\ast\chi(\varphi_{j})\mathrm{d}{\bm{\varphi}})^{2}}{\int P(\mathbf{y}|{\bm{\varphi}})\prod_{k=1}^{p}\mathcal{N}_{\tau}\ast g(\varphi_{k})\mathrm{d}{\bm{\varphi}}}\mathrm{d}\mathbf{y}

Proof.

Let
\left\langle\cdot\right\rangle_{g}
denote the posterior average under

P_{g}({\bm{\varphi}}\mid\mathbf{y})
, i.e., for any
f:\mathbb{R}^{p}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
,

\displaystyle\left\langle f\right\rangle_{g}=\frac{\int e^{-\frac{1}{2}(\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{X}{\bm{\varphi}})^{\top}{\bm{\Sigma}}^{-1}(\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{X}{\bm{\varphi}})+\sum_{j=1}^{p}\log\mathcal{N}_{\tau}\ast g(\varphi_{j})}f({\bm{\varphi}})\mathrm{d}{\bm{\varphi}}}{\int e^{-\frac{1}{2}(\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{X}{\bm{\varphi}})^{\top}{\bm{\Sigma}}^{-1}(\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{X}{\bm{\varphi}})+\sum_{j=1}^{p}\log\mathcal{N}_{\tau}\ast g(\varphi_{j})}\mathrm{d}{\bm{\varphi}}}.

Then differentiating (
2.10
) yields that

\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\bar{F}(g_{t})=-\frac{1}{p}\left\langle\sum_{j=1}^{p}\frac{\mathcal{N}_{\tau}\ast\chi}{\mathcal{N}_{\tau}\ast g_{t}}(\varphi_{j})\right\rangle_{g_{t}},

and

\frac{\mathrm{d}^{2}}{\mathrm{d}t^{2}}\bar{F}_{n}(g_{t})=\frac{1}{p}\left[\left\langle\sum_{j=1}^{p}\frac{\mathcal{N}_{\tau}\ast\chi}{\mathcal{N}_{\tau}\ast g_{t}}(\varphi_{j})\right\rangle^{2}_{g_{t}}+\left\langle\sum_{j=1}^{p}\Big(\frac{\mathcal{N}_{\tau}\ast\chi}{\mathcal{N}_{\tau}\ast g_{t}}\Big)^{2}(\varphi_{j})\right\rangle_{g_{t}}-\left\langle\Big(\sum_{j=1}^{p}\frac{\mathcal{N}_{\tau}\ast\chi}{\mathcal{N}_{\tau}\ast g_{t}}(\varphi_{j})\Big)^{2}\right\rangle_{g_{t}}\right],

as desired.
At
t=0
and
g_{0}=g
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{g}\left[\left\langle\sum_{j=1}^{p}\frac{\mathcal{N}_{\tau}\ast\chi}{\mathcal{N}_{\tau}\ast g}(\theta_{j})\right\rangle^{2}_{g}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{g}\left[\bigg(\sum_{j=1}^{p}\frac{\int P(\mathbf{y}|{\bm{\varphi}})\prod_{k:k\neq j}\mathcal{N}_{\tau}\ast g(\varphi_{k})\cdot\mathcal{N}_{\tau}\ast\chi(\varphi_{j})\mathrm{d}{\bm{\varphi}}}{\int P(\mathbf{y}|{\bm{\varphi}})\prod_{k=1}^{p}\mathcal{N}_{\tau}\ast g(\varphi_{k})\mathrm{d}{\bm{\varphi}}}\bigg)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=\int\frac{(\sum_{j=1}^{p}\int P(\mathbf{y}|{\bm{\varphi}})\prod_{k:k\neq j}\mathcal{N}_{\tau}\ast g(\varphi_{k})\cdot\mathcal{N}_{\tau}\ast\chi(\varphi_{j})\mathrm{d}{\bm{\varphi}})^{2}}{\int P(\mathbf{y}|{\bm{\varphi}})\prod_{k=1}^{p}\mathcal{N}_{\tau}\ast g(\varphi_{k})\mathrm{d}{\bm{\varphi}}}\mathrm{d}\mathbf{y},

and, by the tower property of conditional expectation and independence of

(\varphi_{1},\ldots,\varphi_{p})
under the prior,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{g}\left\langle\sum_{j=1}^{p}\Big(\frac{\mathcal{N}_{\tau}\ast\chi}{\mathcal{N}_{\tau}\ast g}\Big)^{2}(\varphi_{j})\right\rangle_{g}=\mathbb{E}_{g}\left[\sum_{j=1}^{p}\Big(\frac{\mathcal{N}_{\tau}\ast\chi}{\mathcal{N}_{\tau}\ast g}\Big)^{2}(\varphi_{j})\right]=\mathbb{E}_{g}\left\langle\Big(\sum_{j=1}^{p}\frac{\mathcal{N}_{\tau}\ast\chi}{\mathcal{N}_{\tau}\ast g}(\varphi_{j})\Big)^{2}\right\rangle_{g},

as desired.
∎

We write
\bar{H}_{n}[g](\chi)(\mathbf{y})
to make explicit the dependence of
\bar{H}_{n}[g](\chi)
on the observed data
\mathbf{y}
.
The following lemma establishes regularity of the function
\bar{H}_{n}[g](\chi)
. We present its proof after the main proof.

Lemma B.6
.

1.

The envelope function
\mathsf{F}(\mathbf{y})=\sup_{g\in\mathcal{P}(M),\chi\in\Gamma}\bar{H}_{n}[g](\chi)(\mathbf{y})
satisfies
\mathbb{E}_{g_{\ast}}\mathsf{F}(\mathbf{y})<\infty
.

2.

For any
\mathbf{y}
, the map
(g,\chi)\mapsto\bar{H}_{n}[g](\chi)(\mathbf{y})
is jointly
continuous on
\mathcal{P}(M)\times\Gamma
, with respect to the weak topology for
g\in\mathcal{P}(M)

and the TV norm
\lVert\chi\rVert_{\tv}=\int_{-M}^{M}|\chi(\mathrm{d}\theta)|
for
\chi\in\Gamma
.

Proof of Proposition
3.8
.

Let
H_{n}(g)\equiv\inf_{\chi\in\Gamma}\bar{H}_{n}[g](\chi)
and
H(g)
be defined similarly. Since
\Gamma
is compact, condition (
3.20
) yields that
H(g_{\ast})>0
. Since
\bar{H}_{n}[g](\chi)
is jointly continuous in
(g,\chi)
by Lemma
B.6
-(2),
\bar{H}[g](\chi)
is also jointly continuous by the uniform convergence of
\{\bar{H}_{n}[g](\chi)\}_{n}
in (
3.19
).
Then since
\Gamma
is compact,
H(g)
is also continuous in
g
.
Recall the definition of
d_{v}
in (
A.1
), which metrizes weak
convergence
[
GG20
]
. Then, fixing a constant
v>0
to be specified
later, there exists some
(n,p)
-independent
\delta>0
such that
H(g)>0

whenever
g\in B_{v}(\delta;g_{\ast})=\{\bar{g}\in\mathcal{P}(M):d_{v}(\bar{g},g_{\ast})\leq\delta\}
. For any
g\in B_{v}(\delta;g_{*})
,
if
\chi_{0}=\arg\,\min_{\chi\in\Gamma}\bar{H}[g](\chi)
, then

\displaystyle H_{n}(g)-H(g)=\min_{\chi\in\Gamma}\bar{H}_{n}[g](\chi)-\bar{H}[g](\chi_{0})\leq\bar{H}_{n}[g](\chi_{0})-\bar{H}[g](\chi_{0}).

An analogous lower bound concludes

\displaystyle\sup_{g\in B_{v}(\delta;g_{\ast})}\big|H_{n}(g)-H(g)\big|\leq\sup_{g\in B_{v}(\delta;g_{\ast}),\chi\in\Gamma}|\bar{H}_{n}[g](\chi)-\bar{H}[g](\chi)|.

Hence by (
3.19
),

\displaystyle\sup_{g\in B_{v}(\delta;g_{\ast})}|H_{n}(g)-H(g)|\stackrel{{\scriptstyle P}}{{\rightarrow}}0,

so there exists an event with probability converging to
1
on which

H_{n}(g)\geq H(g)/2>0\qquad\text{ for all }\qquad g\in B_{v}(\delta;g_{\ast}).

(B.22)

Now observe that

B_{v}(\delta;g_{\ast})
is a convex set, by definition of
d_{v}
in
(
A.1
) and convexity of the map
g\mapsto W_{1}(\mathcal{N}_{v}*g_{*},\mathcal{N}_{v}*g)
.
Fix any such
g_{0},g_{1}\in B_{v}(\delta;g_{\ast})
and let
s(t)\equiv\bar{F}_{n}((1-t)g_{0}+tg_{1})
. Then by (
B.22
) and
Lemma
B.5
,

\displaystyle s^{\prime\prime}(t)=\bar{H}_{n}[g_{t}](g_{1}-g_{0})=\bar{H}_{n}[g_{t}]\Big(\frac{g_{1}-g_{0}}{\lVert g_{1}-g_{0}\rVert_{\tv}}\Big)\cdot\lVert g_{1}-g_{0}\rVert_{\tv}^{2}\geq 0,

which establishes the desired convexity for all
g\in B_{v}(\delta;g_{\ast})
.

Lastly, note that since
g_{\ast}
is the unique minimizer of
\bar{F}(g)
, there
exists an
(n,p)
-independent constant
c_{0}>0
such that

\displaystyle\Big\{g:\bar{F}(g)\leq\bar{F}(g_{\ast})+c_{0}\Big\}\subset B_{v}(\delta;g_{\ast}).

Indeed, otherwise there would exist a sequence
\{g_{t}\}
outside
B_{v}(\delta;g_{\ast})
satisfying
\bar{F}(g_{t})\leq\bar{F}(g_{\ast})+t^{-1}
. By weak
compactness of
\mathcal{P}(M)
, this sequence has a limit point
g^{\prime}
, which by weak
continuity of
\bar{F}
must satisfy
\bar{F}(g^{\prime})\leq\bar{F}(g_{\ast})
,
contradicting uniqueness of
g_{\ast}
as the minimizer of
\bar{F}
. By the uniform convergence of
\{\bar{F}_{n}(g)\}_{n}
in (
3.19
), for all large
n,p
it holds

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}_{n}:=\Big\{g:\bar{F}_{n}(g)\leq\bar{F}_{n}(g_{\ast})+c_{0}/2\Big\}\subset\Big\{g:\bar{F}(g)\leq\bar{F}(g_{\ast})+c_{0}\Big\}\subset B_{v}(\delta;g_{\ast}).

Similarly,
\mathcal{K}:=\{g:\bar{F}(g)<\bar{F}(g_{\ast})+c_{0}/4\}\subset\mathcal{K}_{n}
,
where
\mathcal{K}
is a
(n,p)
-independent weak neighborhood of
g_{\ast}
by weak
continuity of
\bar{F}
, as desired.
∎

Proof of Lemma
B.6
.

To prove claim (1), note that for any
\chi\in\Gamma
and
g\in\mathcal{P}(M)
,

\displaystyle\Big|\frac{\mathcal{N}_{\tau}\ast\chi}{\mathcal{N}_{\tau}\ast g}(\varphi_{j})\Big|=\frac{\Big|\int_{-M}^{M}e^{-\frac{u^{2}}{2\tau^{2}}+\frac{\varphi_{j}u}{\tau^{2}}}\chi(u)\mathrm{d}u\Big|}{\int_{-M}^{M}e^{-\frac{u^{2}}{2\tau^{2}}+\frac{\varphi_{j}u}{\tau^{2}}}g(u)\mathrm{d}u}\leq Ce^{C|\varphi_{j}|}\cdot\lVert\chi\rVert_{\tv}\leq Ce^{C\lVert{\bm{\varphi}}\rVert}.

(B.23)

Hence by Lemma
B.7
to follow (with
{\bm{\Gamma}}
and

c({\bm{\theta}})
as defined therein),

\displaystyle\left\langle e^{C\lVert{\bm{\varphi}}\rVert}\right\rangle_{g}=\int_{[-M,M]^{p}}P_{g}({\bm{\theta}}\mid\mathbf{y})\mathbb{E}_{{\bm{\varphi}}\sim\mathcal{N}(c({\bm{\theta}}),{\bm{\Gamma}})}e^{C\lVert{\bm{\varphi}}\rVert}\mathrm{d}\bm{{\bm{\theta}}}\leq C\sup_{{\bm{\theta}}\in[-M,M]^{p}}e^{C\lVert c({\bm{\theta}})\rVert}\leq C^{\prime}e^{C^{\prime}\lVert\mathbf{y}\rVert},

(B.24)

where
C,C^{\prime}
may depend on
(\lVert\mathbf{X}\rVert_{{\operatorname{op}}},\sigma^{2},\tau^{2},p,M)
. Since
\mathbf{y}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}\mathbf{X}{\bm{\theta}}+{\bm{\varepsilon}}
with
\theta_{j}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle iid}}{{\sim}}g_{\ast}

and
\varepsilon_{i}\overset{iid}{\sim}\mathcal{N}(0,\sigma^{2})
,

\sup_{g\in\mathcal{P}(M),\chi\in\Gamma}\big|\frac{\mathcal{N}_{\tau}\ast\chi}{\mathcal{N}_{\tau}\ast g}(\varphi_{j})\big|^{2}
and hence
\mathsf{F}(\mathbf{y})
is integrable, establishing claim (1).

Next we establish claim (2). For presentational simplicity, we only prove the
joint continuity of the first term of
\bar{H}_{n}[g](\chi)(\mathbf{y})
in (
3.18
), and that of the other two terms follows similarly. It suffices to prove the continuity for the first summand there, which is

\displaystyle H(g,\chi;\mathbf{y})

\displaystyle\equiv\left\langle\Big(\frac{\mathcal{N}_{\tau}\ast\chi}{\mathcal{N}_{\tau}\ast g}\Big)^{2}(\varphi_{1})\right\rangle_{g}=\frac{\int P(\mathbf{y}|{\bm{\varphi}})\prod_{j=1}^{p}\mathcal{N}_{\tau}\ast g(\varphi_{j})\cdot\Big(\frac{\mathcal{N}_{\tau}\ast\chi}{\mathcal{N}_{\tau}\ast g}\Big)^{2}(\varphi_{1})\mathrm{d}{\bm{\varphi}}}{P_{g}(\mathbf{y})}.

For two pairs
(g_{i},\chi_{i})
,
i=1,2
,
|H(g_{1},\chi_{1})-H(g_{2},\chi_{2})|\leq\sum_{i=1}^{4}(I_{i})
, where

\displaystyle(I_{1})

\displaystyle=\Big(\frac{1}{P_{g_{1}}(\mathbf{y})}-\frac{1}{P_{g_{2}}(\mathbf{y})}\Big)\int P(\mathbf{y}|{\bm{\varphi}})\prod_{j=1}^{p}\mathcal{N}_{\tau}\ast g_{1}(\varphi_{j})\cdot\Big(\frac{\mathcal{N}_{\tau}\ast\chi_{1}}{\mathcal{N}_{\tau}\ast g_{1}}\Big)^{2}(\varphi_{1})\mathrm{d}{\bm{\varphi}},

\displaystyle(I_{2})

\displaystyle=P_{g_{2}}(\mathbf{y})^{-1}\int P(\mathbf{y}|{\bm{\varphi}})\Big(\prod_{j=1}^{p}\mathcal{N}_{\tau}\ast g_{1}(\varphi_{j})-\prod_{j=1}^{p}\mathcal{N}_{\tau}\ast g_{2}(\varphi_{j})\Big)\cdot\Big(\frac{\mathcal{N}_{\tau}\ast\chi_{1}}{\mathcal{N}_{\tau}\ast g_{1}}\Big)^{2}(\varphi_{1})\mathrm{d}{\bm{\varphi}},

\displaystyle(I_{3})

\displaystyle=P_{g_{2}}(\mathbf{y})^{-1}\int\prod_{j=1}^{p}\mathcal{N}_{\tau}\ast g_{2}(\varphi_{j})\cdot\bigg[\Big(\frac{\mathcal{N}_{\tau}\ast\chi_{1}}{\mathcal{N}_{\tau}\ast g_{1}}\Big)^{2}(\varphi_{1})-\Big(\frac{\mathcal{N}_{\tau}\ast\chi_{1}}{\mathcal{N}_{\tau}\ast g_{2}}\Big)^{2}(\varphi_{1})\bigg]\mathrm{d}{\bm{\varphi}},

\displaystyle(I_{4})

\displaystyle=P_{g_{2}}(\mathbf{y})^{-1}\int P(\mathbf{y}|{\bm{\varphi}})\prod_{j=1}^{p}\mathcal{N}_{\tau}\ast g_{2}(\varphi_{j})\cdot\frac{\big(\mathcal{N}_{\tau}\ast\chi_{1}\big)^{2}(\varphi_{1})-\big(\mathcal{N}_{\tau}\ast\chi_{2}\big)^{2}(\varphi_{1})}{\big(\mathcal{N}_{\tau}\ast g_{2}\big)^{2}(\varphi_{1})}\mathrm{d}{\bm{\varphi}}.

We will show that
(I_{1})
-
(I_{4})
converge to 0 when
(g_{1},\chi_{1})
converges to

(g_{2},\chi_{2})
under the weak topology for
g
and TV norm for
\chi
.

Bound on
(I_{1})
For
i=1,2
, let
\mu_{i}
denote the law of

\mathbf{X}{\bm{\theta}}
on
\mathbb{R}^{n}
with
\theta_{j}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle iid}}{{\sim}}g_{i}
, then
\mu_{i}
is a measure supported in the Euclidean ball
B_{n}(0,\sqrt{p}\lVert\mathbf{X}\rVert_{{\operatorname{op}}}M)
. It is clear that
\mu_{1}
converges weakly to
\mu_{2}
if
g_{1}
converges weakly to
g_{2}
. Using

\displaystyle P_{g_{1}}(\mathbf{y})-P_{g_{2}}(\mathbf{y})=(2\pi\sigma^{2})^{-\frac{n}{2}}\int e^{-\frac{\lVert\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{u}\rVert^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}}}(\mu_{1}-\mu_{2})(\mathrm{d}\mathbf{u}),

we have

\displaystyle(I_{1})

\displaystyle=\frac{P_{g_{2}}(\mathbf{y})-P_{g_{1}}(\mathbf{y})}{P_{g_{2}}(\mathbf{y})}\left\langle\Big(\frac{\mathcal{N}_{\tau}\ast\chi_{1}}{\mathcal{N}_{\tau}\ast g_{1}}\Big)^{2}(\varphi_{1})\right\rangle_{g_{1}}=(2\pi\sigma^{2})^{-\frac{n}{2}}\int\mathsf{f}(\mathbf{u})(\mu_{2}-\mu_{1})(\mathrm{d}\mathbf{u}),

where

\displaystyle\mathsf{f}(\mathbf{u})=\frac{\left\langle\Big(\frac{\mathcal{N}_{\tau}\ast\chi_{1}}{\mathcal{N}_{\tau}\ast g_{1}}\Big)^{2}(\varphi_{1})\right\rangle_{g_{1}}}{P_{g_{2}}(\mathbf{y})}e^{-\frac{\lVert\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{u}\rVert^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}}}.

By (
B.23
)-(
B.24
),
\big|\left\langle\Big(\frac{\mathcal{N}_{\tau}\ast\chi_{1}}{\mathcal{N}_{\tau}\ast g_{1}}\Big)^{2}(\varphi_{1})\right\rangle_{g_{1}}\big|\leq C
, and similarly
P_{g_{2}}(\mathbf{y})\geq C
, where the constant
C
may depend on
\mathbf{y}
but does not depend on
(g_{i},\chi_{i})
. Hence
\mathsf{f}(\mathbf{u})
admits an upper bound independent of
(g_{i},\chi_{i})
, and
(I_{1})\rightarrow 0
as
(g_{1},\chi_{1})
converges to
(g_{2},\chi_{2})
.

Bound on
(I_{2})
We have by telescoping

\displaystyle(I_{2})\leq\frac{1}{P_{g_{2}}(\mathbf{y})}\cdot\sum_{j=1}^{p}\int P(\mathbf{y}|{\bm{\varphi}})\mathcal{N}_{\tau}\ast(g_{1}-g_{2})(\varphi_{j})\prod_{k:k\neq j}\mathcal{N}_{\tau}\ast\widetilde{g}(\varphi_{k})\cdot\Big(\frac{\mathcal{N}_{\tau}\ast\chi_{1}}{\mathcal{N}_{\tau}\ast g_{1}}\Big)^{2}(\varphi_{1})\mathrm{d}{\bm{\varphi}},

where
\widetilde{g}\in\{g_{1},g_{2}\}
. Fix any
j\in[p]
, and note that the
j
th term equals some constant multiple of
\int\mathsf{f}(u)(g_{1}-g_{2})(\mathrm{d}u)
, where

\displaystyle\mathsf{f}(u)

\displaystyle=\int P(\mathbf{y}|{\bm{\varphi}})e^{-\frac{(\varphi_{j}-u)^{2}}{2\tau^{2}}}\prod_{k:k\neq j}\mathcal{N}_{\tau}\ast\widetilde{g}(\varphi_{k})\cdot\Big(\frac{\mathcal{N}_{\tau}\ast\chi_{1}}{\mathcal{N}_{\tau}\ast g_{1}}\Big)^{2}(\varphi_{1})\mathrm{d}{\bm{\varphi}}

\displaystyle\leq C\int e^{C|\varphi_{1}|}\prod_{k:k\neq j}\mathcal{N}_{\tau}\ast\widetilde{g}(\varphi_{k})\cdot e^{-\frac{(\varphi_{j}-u)^{2}}{2\tau^{2}}}\mathrm{d}{\bm{\varphi}}\leq Ce^{C|u|},

using (
B.23
)-(
B.24
). This and the lower bound of
P_{g_{2}}(\mathbf{y})
above imply
\mathsf{f}(u)
is bounded on
[-M,M]
independently of
(g_{i},\chi_{i})
, and hence
(I_{2})\rightarrow 0
as
(g_{1},\chi_{1})
converges to
(g_{2},\chi_{2})
.

Bound on
(I_{3})
Note that
(I_{3})
equals a constant multiple of
\int\mathsf{f}(u)(g_{1}-g_{2})(\mathrm{d}u)
, where

\displaystyle\mathsf{f}(u)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{P_{g_{2}}(\mathbf{y})}\int P(\mathbf{y}|{\bm{\varphi}})\prod_{j=1}^{p}\mathcal{N}_{\tau}\ast g_{2}(\varphi_{j})\frac{(\mathcal{N}_{\tau}\ast\chi)^{2}(\varphi_{1})\cdot[\mathcal{N}_{\tau}\ast(g_{1}+g_{2})](\varphi_{1})}{(\mathcal{N}_{\tau}\ast g_{1})^{2}(\varphi_{1})(\mathcal{N}_{\tau}\ast g_{2})^{2}(\varphi_{1})}e^{-\frac{(\varphi_{1}-u)^{2}}{2\tau^{2}}}\mathrm{d}{\bm{\varphi}}

\displaystyle\leq\int Ce^{C|\varphi_{1}|}e^{-\frac{(\varphi_{1}-u)^{2}}{2\tau^{2}}}\mathrm{d}\varphi_{1}\leq Ce^{C|u|},

yielding
(I_{3})\rightarrow 0
when
(g_{1},\chi_{1})
converges to
(g_{2},\chi_{2})
.

Bound on
(I_{4})
Note that
(I_{4})
is bounded by a constant multiple of
\int|\mathsf{f}(u)||(\chi_{1}-\chi_{2})(\mathrm{d}u)|
, where

\displaystyle\mathsf{f}(u)=\frac{1}{P_{g_{2}}(\mathbf{y})}\int P(\mathbf{y}|{\bm{\varphi}})\prod_{j=1}^{p}\mathcal{N}_{\tau}\ast g_{2}(\varphi_{j})\cdot\frac{\big[\mathcal{N}_{\tau}\ast(\chi_{1}+\chi_{2})\big](\varphi_{1})}{\big(\mathcal{N}_{\tau}\ast g_{2}\big)^{2}(\varphi_{1})}e^{-\frac{(\varphi_{1}-u)^{2}}{2\tau^{2}}}\mathrm{d}{\bm{\varphi}}\leq Ce^{C|u|},

using the same argument as
(I_{3})
. Combining the bounds for
(I_{1})
-
(I_{4})
completes the proof for claim (2).
∎

Lemma B.7
.

For any density
g(\cdot)
on
[-M,M]
, it holds that

\displaystyle P_{g}({\bm{\varphi}}\mid\mathbf{y})

\displaystyle=\int_{[-M,M]^{p}}\phi_{\Gamma}({\bm{\varphi}}-c({\bm{\theta}}))P_{g}({\bm{\theta}}\mid\mathbf{y})\mathrm{d}{\bm{\theta}},

where
\phi_{\Gamma}(\cdot)
denotes the density of
\mathcal{N}(0,\Gamma)
on
\mathbb{R}^{p}
, and

\displaystyle\Gamma

\displaystyle=\frac{\tau^{2}}{\sigma^{2}}(\sigma^{2}\operatorname{Id}-\tau^{2}\mathbf{X}^{\top}\mathbf{X}),\quad c({\bm{\theta}})=\frac{\tau^{2}}{\sigma^{2}}\mathbf{X}^{\top}\mathbf{y}+{\bm{\theta}}-\frac{\tau^{2}}{\sigma^{2}}\mathbf{X}^{\top}\mathbf{X}{\bm{\theta}}.

Proof.

We have

\displaystyle P_{g}({\bm{\varphi}}\mid\mathbf{y})=\int P_{g}({\bm{\varphi}},{\bm{\theta}}\mid\mathbf{y})\mathrm{d}{\bm{\theta}}=\int P_{g}({\bm{\varphi}}\mid{\bm{\theta}},\mathbf{y})P_{g}({\bm{\theta}}\mid\mathbf{y})\mathrm{d}{\bm{\theta}},

and direct calculation yields that
P_{g}({\bm{\varphi}}\mid{\bm{\theta}},\mathbf{y})=\mathcal{N}(c({\bm{\theta}}),{\bm{\Gamma}})
with
{\bm{\Gamma}}=(\mathbf{X}^{\top}{\bm{\Sigma}}^{-1}\mathbf{X}+\tau^{-2}\operatorname{Id})^{-1}
and
c({\bm{\theta}})={\bm{\Gamma}}\big(\mathbf{X}^{\top}{\bm{\Sigma}}^{-1}\mathbf{y}+\tau^{-2}{\bm{\theta}}\big)
. To simplify the expression for
{\bm{\Gamma}}
, recall that the
Woodbury matrix identity reads
(A+UCV)^{-1}=A^{-1}-A^{-1}U(C^{-1}+VA^{-1}U)^{-1}VA^{-1}
, provided that
A
is invertible. Apply this formula with

A=\tau^{-2}\operatorname{Id}
,
U=\mathbf{X}^{\top}
,
C={\bm{\Sigma}}^{-1}
,
V=\mathbf{X}
yields that

\displaystyle{\bm{\Gamma}}=\tau^{2}\operatorname{Id}-\tau^{4}\mathbf{X}^{\top}({\bm{\Sigma}}+\tau^{2}\mathbf{X}\mathbf{X}^{\top})^{-1}\mathbf{X}=\tau^{2}\operatorname{Id}-\frac{\tau^{4}}{\sigma^{2}}\mathbf{X}^{\top}\mathbf{X}=\frac{\tau^{2}}{\sigma^{2}}(\sigma^{2}\operatorname{Id}-\tau^{2}\mathbf{X}^{\top}\mathbf{X}),

as desired. We can then simplify
c({\bm{\theta}})
as

\displaystyle c({\bm{\theta}})

\displaystyle=\frac{\tau^{2}}{\sigma^{2}}(\sigma^{2}\operatorname{Id}-\tau^{2}\mathbf{X}^{\top}\mathbf{X})\mathbf{X}^{\top}{\bm{\Sigma}}^{-1}\mathbf{y}+\frac{1}{\sigma^{2}}(\sigma^{2}-\tau^{2}\mathbf{X}^{\top}\mathbf{X}){\bm{\theta}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\tau^{2}}{\sigma^{2}}\mathbf{X}^{\top}(\sigma^{2}\operatorname{Id}-\tau^{2}\mathbf{X}\mathbf{X}^{\top}){\bm{\Sigma}}^{-1}\mathbf{y}+{\bm{\theta}}-\frac{\tau^{2}}{\sigma^{2}}\mathbf{X}^{\top}\mathbf{X}{\bm{\theta}}=\frac{\tau^{2}}{\sigma^{2}}\mathbf{X}^{\top}\mathbf{y}+{\bm{\theta}}-\frac{\tau^{2}}{\sigma^{2}}\mathbf{X}^{\top}\mathbf{X}{\bm{\theta}}.

∎

Finally we illustrate a simple setting where the assumptions of
Proposition
3.8
hold, where
\mathbf{X}
is a block design.

Proposition B.8
.

Suppose
\mathbf{X}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times p}
is block diagonal with
N
identical blocks

\bar{\mathbf{X}}\in\mathbb{R}^{B_{1}\times B_{2}}
, where
\bar{\mathbf{X}}
and
(B_{1},B_{2})
are fixed
independent of
(n,p)
,
\bar{\mathbf{X}}\neq 0
, and
(n,p)=(NB_{1},NB_{2})
.
Then there exists a set in
\mathbb{R}^{B_{1}\times B_{2}}
of full Lebesgue measure such that for every
\bar{\mathbf{X}}
in this set, the assumptions of Proposition
3.8
are satisfied with
\bar{F}=\mathbb{E}\bar{F}_{n}
and
\bar{H}=\mathbb{E}\bar{H}_{n}
.

Proof.

We verify uniform convergence of
\bar{H}_{n}
to
\bar{H}
; a similar argument
holds for
\bar{F}_{n}
, which we omit for brevity.
Partition the observation
\mathbf{y}=(\bar{\mathbf{y}}^{1},\ldots,\bar{\mathbf{y}}^{N})
according to the
blocks, with
\bar{\mathbf{y}}^{i}\in\mathbb{R}^{B_{1}}
, so that each block is generated by

\displaystyle\bar{\mathbf{y}}^{i}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}\bar{\mathbf{X}}\bar{{\bm{\varphi}}}^{i}+\bar{{\bm{\varepsilon}}}^{i},\quad(\bar{\varphi}_{1}^{i},\ldots,\bar{\varphi}_{B_{2}}^{i})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle iid}}{{\sim}}g_{\ast},\quad\bar{{\bm{\varepsilon}}}^{i}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\bar{{\bm{\Sigma}}})\text{ with }\bar{{\bm{\Sigma}}}=\sigma^{2}\operatorname{Id}_{B_{1}}-\tau^{2}\bar{\mathbf{X}}\bar{\mathbf{X}}^{\top}.

(B.25)

Note that under this block design,

\displaystyle\bar{H}_{n}[g](\chi)=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathsf{h}_{g,\chi}(\bar{\mathbf{y}}^{i})\quad\text{ and }\quad\bar{H}[g](\chi)=\mathbb{E}_{g_{\ast}}\bar{H}_{n}[g](\chi)=\mathbb{E}_{g_{\ast}}\mathsf{h}_{g,\chi}(\bar{\mathbf{y}}),

(B.26)

where, with the notation

\displaystyle\left\langle f\right\rangle_{g}=\frac{\int f(\bar{{\bm{\varphi}}})e^{-\frac{1}{2}(\bar{\mathbf{y}}-\bar{\mathbf{X}}\bar{{\bm{\varphi}}})^{\top}\bar{{\bm{\Sigma}}}^{-1}(\bar{\mathbf{y}}-\bar{\mathbf{X}}\bar{{\bm{\varphi}}})+\sum_{j=1}^{B_{2}}\log\mathcal{N}_{\tau}\ast g(\bar{\varphi}_{j})}\mathrm{d}\bar{{\bm{\varphi}}}}{\int e^{-\frac{1}{2}(\bar{y}-\bar{\mathbf{X}}\bar{{\bm{\varphi}}})^{\top}\bar{{\bm{\Sigma}}}^{-1}(\bar{\mathbf{y}}-\bar{\mathbf{X}}\bar{{\bm{\varphi}}})+\sum_{j=1}^{B_{2}}\log\mathcal{N}_{\tau}\ast g(\bar{\varphi}_{j})}\mathrm{d}\bar{{\bm{\varphi}}}},\quad f:\mathbb{R}^{B_{2}}\rightarrow\mathbb{R},

we define

\displaystyle\mathsf{h}_{g,\chi}(\bar{\mathbf{y}})=\frac{1}{B_{2}}\bigg[\left\langle\sum_{j=1}^{B_{2}}\Big(\frac{\mathcal{N}_{\tau}\ast\chi}{\mathcal{N}_{\tau}\ast g}(\bar{\varphi}_{j})\Big)^{2}\right\rangle_{g}-\left\langle\Big(\sum_{j=1}^{B_{2}}\frac{\mathcal{N}_{\tau}\ast\chi}{\mathcal{N}_{\tau}\ast g}(\bar{\varphi}_{j})\Big)^{2}\right\rangle_{g}+\left\langle\sum_{j=1}^{B_{2}}\frac{\mathcal{N}_{\tau}\ast\chi}{\mathcal{N}_{\tau}\ast g}(\bar{\varphi}_{j})\right\rangle^{2}_{g}\bigg].

(B.27)

It is clear that
\bar{H}[g](\chi)
is an
(n,p)
-independent function.
To show uniform convergence of
\bar{H}_{n}[g](\chi)
,
it suffices to establish that

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}\equiv\bigg\{\bar{\mathbf{y}}\mapsto\mathsf{h}_{g,\chi}(\bar{\mathbf{y}}):g\in\mathcal{P}(M),\chi\in\Gamma\bigg\}

is Glivenko-Cantelli with respect to the measure
P_{g_{\ast}}(\bar{\mathbf{y}})
. Since
the parameter space
\Theta\equiv\{(g,\chi):g\in\mathcal{P}(M),\chi\in\Gamma\}

is compact, a sufficient condition (see
[
Wel05
, Lemma 6.1]

and
[
VW23
, Theorem 2.4.1]
) is to show: (i) For every

\bar{\mathbf{y}}
,
(g,\chi)\mapsto\mathsf{h}_{g,\chi}(\bar{\mathbf{y}})
is jointly
continuous; (ii) the envelope function
\mathsf{F}(\bar{\mathbf{y}})\equiv\sup_{(g,\chi)\in\Theta}\mathsf{h}_{g,\chi}(\bar{\mathbf{y}})
is

P_{g_{\ast}}
-integrable. These conditions are established by
Lemma
B.6
, so uniform convergence follows.

Next we show
g_{\ast}
is the unique global minimizer of
\bar{F}(g)
, whenever
\bar{\mathbf{X}}
contains a row
\mathbf{x}_{i_{0}}\in\mathbb{R}^{B_{2}}
such that

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{B_{2}}x_{i_{0},j}^{q}\neq 0,\quad\text{ for all }q=1,2,3,\ldots.

(B.28)

It is clear that the set of such
\bar{\mathbf{X}}
has full Lebesgue measure.
Since
\bar{F}(g)=D_{\mathrm{KL}}(P_{g_{\ast}}(\bar{\mathbf{y}})||P_{g}(\bar{\mathbf{y}}))
, it suffices to show that the map
g\mapsto P_{g}(\bar{\mathbf{y}})
(the
image as a distribution) is injective. Since the reduced model
(
B.25
) is equivalent to
\bar{\mathbf{y}}=\bar{\mathbf{X}}\bar{{\bm{\theta}}}+\mathcal{N}(0,\sigma^{2}\operatorname{Id}_{B_{1}})
, it suffices to show
g\mapsto P_{g}(\bar{\mathbf{X}}\bar{{\bm{\theta}}})
is injective. We prove by contradiction. Suppose there are
g,\widetilde{g}
such that
\bar{\mathbf{X}}\bar{{\bm{\theta}}}
has the same distribution when the components of
\bar{{\bm{\theta}}}
are drawn i.i.d. from
g,\widetilde{g}
, then
\mathbf{x}_{i_{0}}^{\top}\bar{{\bm{\theta}}}
also has the same distribution. Let
m_{q}(g)
and
m_{q}(\widetilde{g})
denote the
q
th moment of
g,\widetilde{g}
respectively. We show by induction that
m_{q}(g)=m_{q}(\widetilde{g})
for all
q\geq 1
. The base case
q=1
holds because

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{g}\mathbf{x}_{i_{0}}^{\top}\bar{{\bm{\theta}}}=m_{1}(g)\cdot\sum_{j=1}^{B_{2}}x_{i_{0},j},\quad\mathbb{E}_{\widetilde{g}}\mathbf{x}_{i_{0}}^{\top}\bar{{\bm{\theta}}}=m_{1}(\widetilde{g})\cdot\sum_{j=1}^{B_{2}}x_{i_{0},j},

and the common factor
\sum_{j=1}^{B_{2}}x_{i_{0},j}
is not zero by (
B.28
). Suppose the moments match up to order
q
, then

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{g}\big(\mathbf{x}_{i_{0}}^{\top}\bar{{\bm{\theta}}}\big)^{q+1}=m_{q+1}(g)\cdot\sum_{j=1}^{p}x_{i_{0},j}^{q+1}+R_{q},

where
R_{q}
is some term that only depends on the first
q
moments of
g
. In view of (
B.28
), it holds
m_{q+1}(g)=m_{q+1}(\widetilde{g})
, completing the induction. The conclusion now follows because boundedness of
\mathbf{x}_{i_{0}}^{\top}\bar{{\bm{\theta}}}
implies that its distribution is completely determined by its moments (
[
Bil95
, Theorem 30.1]
).

Finally let us check the lower bound (
3.20
).
Note that
\bar{H}_{n}[g](\chi)\geq 0
by definition, so also
\bar{H}[g](\chi)\geq 0
, and it suffices to show
\bar{H}[g_{*}](\chi)\neq 0
for all
\chi\in\Gamma
.
Suppose by contradiction that
\bar{H}[g_{\ast}](\chi)=0
for some
\chi\neq 0
. Then Lemma
B.5
implies that with probability 1 over

\bar{\mathbf{y}}
,

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{B_{2}}\int P(\bar{\mathbf{y}}|\bar{{\bm{\varphi}}})\prod_{k:k\neq j}\mathcal{N}_{\tau}\ast g_{\ast}(\bar{\varphi}_{k})\cdot\mathcal{N}_{\tau}\ast\chi(\bar{\varphi}_{j})\mathrm{d}\bar{{\bm{\varphi}}}=0.

Take the same
i_{0}
as in (
B.28
). Then, integrating over
the variables
\{y_{i}:i\neq i_{0}\}
, the above identity yields that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{B_{2}}\sum_{j=1}^{B_{2}}\int P(\bar{y}_{i_{0}}|\bar{{\bm{\varphi}}})\prod_{k:k\neq j}\mathcal{N}_{\tau}\ast g_{\ast}(\bar{\varphi}_{k})\cdot\mathcal{N}_{\tau}\ast\chi(\bar{\varphi}_{j})\mathrm{d}\bar{\bm{\varphi}}=0,

or equivalently,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{B_{2}}\sum_{j=1}^{B_{2}}\int P(\bar{y}_{i_{0}}|\bar{{\bm{\theta}}})\prod_{k:k\neq j}g_{\ast}(\bar{\theta}_{k})\cdot\chi(\bar{\theta}_{j})\mathrm{d}\bar{\bm{\theta}}=0.

Let
h_{+}
and
h_{-}
be positive measures such that
\chi=h_{+}-h_{-}
. Since
\int\chi=0
, we have
\int h_{+}=\int h_{-}
. Let
\widetilde{h}_{+}=h_{+}/\int h_{+}
and
\widetilde{h}_{-}=h_{-}/\int h_{-}
be the normalized probability measures. Then the above identity yields that

\displaystyle\int\frac{1}{B_{2}}\sum_{j=1}^{B_{2}}P(\bar{y}_{i_{0}}|\bar{{\bm{\theta}}})\prod_{k:k\neq j}g_{\ast}(\bar{\theta}_{k})\cdot\widetilde{h}_{+}(\bar{\theta}_{j})\mathrm{d}\bar{\bm{\theta}}=\int\frac{1}{B_{2}}\sum_{j=1}^{B_{2}}P(\bar{y}_{i_{0}}|\bar{{\bm{\theta}}})\prod_{k:k\neq j}g_{\ast}(\bar{\theta}_{k})\cdot\widetilde{h}_{-}(\bar{\theta}_{j})\mathrm{d}\bar{\bm{\theta}}.

Note that (
B.25
) is equivalent to
y_{i_{0}}|\bar{{\bm{\theta}}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}\mathbf{x}_{i_{0}}^{\top}\bar{{\bm{\theta}}}+\mathcal{N}(0,\sigma^{2})
, so this implies that the distribution of
\mathbf{x}_{i_{0}}^{\top}\bar{{\bm{\theta}}}
is the same under the following two priors on
\bar{{\bm{\theta}}}
:

\displaystyle P:J\sim\text{Unif}([B_{2}]),\quad(\bar{{\bm{\theta}}}|J=j)\sim\prod_{k:k\neq j}g_{\ast}(\bar{\theta}_{k})\cdot\widetilde{h}_{+}(\theta_{k}),

\displaystyle Q:J\sim\text{Unif}([B_{2}]),\quad(\bar{{\bm{\theta}}}|J=j)\sim\prod_{k:k\neq j}g_{\ast}(\bar{\theta}_{k})\cdot\widetilde{h}_{-}(\theta_{k}).

Let
m_{q}(\widetilde{h}_{+})
and
m_{q}(\widetilde{h}_{-})
denote the
q^{\text{th}}
moment of

\widetilde{h}_{+}
and
\widetilde{h}_{-}
. Then induction again implies
m_{q}(\widetilde{h}_{+})=m_{q}(\widetilde{h}_{-})
under (
B.28
). Indeed, the base case follows from the same argument as above, and for the induction step,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{P}(\mathbf{x}_{i_{0}}^{\top}\bar{{\bm{\theta}}})^{q+1}=m_{q+1}(\widetilde{h}_{+})\cdot\frac{1}{B_{2}}\sum_{j=1}^{p}x_{i_{0},j}^{q+1}+R_{q},

where
R_{q}
is again some term that only depends on the first
q
moments of
\widetilde{h}_{+}
, concluding the induction. Since the distributions of
\widetilde{h}_{+},\widetilde{h}_{-}
are determined by their moments (
[
Bil95
, Theorem 30.1]
), the above arguments yield
h_{+}=h_{-}
and hence
\chi\equiv 0
, leading to a contradiction.
∎

Appendix C
Solution properties of gradient flows

In this appendix, we prove Theorem
3.6
.
Throughout this appendix,
n,p,\mathbf{X},\mathbf{y}
are fixed, and
C,C^{\prime},C_{0},K
etc. denote
constants that may depend on
n,p,\mathbf{X},\mathbf{y}
unless otherwise specified.

By a solution
\{q_{t}(\cdot),g_{t}(\cdot)\}_{t\in[0,T]}
to
(
2.15
–
2.16
), we mean functions

t\mapsto g_{t}(\theta)
in
C^{1}([0,T])
for each
\theta\in[-M,M]
and

(t,{\bm{\varphi}})\mapsto q_{t}({\bm{\varphi}})
in
C^{1,2}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{p})
,
such that
q_{t},g_{t}
are probability densities for all
t\in[0,T]
and
(
2.15
–
2.16
) hold in the classical sense of pointwise
equality. We call the solution unique if for any other such solution

\{\widetilde{q}_{t},\widetilde{g}_{t}\}_{t\in[0,T]}
, we have

q_{t}({\bm{\varphi}})=\widetilde{q}_{t}({\bm{\varphi}})
and
g_{t}(\theta)=\widetilde{g}_{t}(\theta)

for all
t\in[0,T]
,
\theta\in[-M,M]
, and
{\bm{\varphi}}\in\mathbb{R}^{p}
.

We equip
\mathcal{P}(M)
with the total variation metric

d_{\mathrm{TV}}(g,\widetilde{g})=\sup_{A\subseteq[-M,M]}\big|g(A)-\widetilde{g}(A)\big|
,
or equivalently

d_{\mathrm{TV}}(g,\widetilde{g})=\frac{1}{2}\int_{-M}^{M}|g(\theta)-\widetilde{g}(\theta)|\mathrm{d}\theta
when
g,\widetilde{g}\in\mathcal{P}_{*}(M)
both admit densities. Then
\mathcal{P}(M)
is
complete under
d_{\mathrm{TV}}
. For any
T>0
, let

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{g}\equiv\mathcal{L}_{g}(T)=C\big([0,T],\mathcal{P}(M)\big),

the space of continuous (with respect to
d_{\mathrm{TV}}
)
\mathcal{P}(M)
-valued processes
\{g_{t}\}_{t\in[0,T]}
,
equipped with the metric

d_{\infty,\mathrm{TV}}(g,\widetilde{g})=\sup_{t\in[0,T]}d_{\mathrm{TV}}(g_{t},\widetilde{g}_{t})
.
Then also
\mathcal{L}_{g}
is complete under
d_{\infty,\mathrm{TV}}
.

Next, let

\displaystyle E_{q}\equiv E_{q}(T)=C([0,T],\mathbb{R}^{p}),

the space of continuous
\mathbb{R}^{p}
-valued sample paths
\{{\bm{\varphi}}_{t}\}_{t\in[0,T]}
,
equipped analogously with
the norm
\|{\bm{\varphi}}\|_{\infty,2}=\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\lVert{\bm{\varphi}}_{t}\rVert_{2}
. Let
\mathcal{L}_{q}\equiv\mathcal{L}_{q}(T)
be the space of probability distributions on
E_{q}

for which
\mathbb{E}[\|{\bm{\varphi}}\|_{\infty,2}^{2}]<\infty
,
equipped with the Wasserstein-2 distance induced by this norm,

\displaystyle d_{W}(q,\widetilde{q})

\displaystyle=\inf_{{\bm{\varphi}}=\{{\bm{\varphi}}_{t}\}_{t\in[0,T]}\sim q,\,\widetilde{\bm{\varphi}}=\{\widetilde{\bm{\varphi}}_{t}\}_{t\in[0,T]}\sim\widetilde{q}}\big(\mathbb{E}\lVert{\bm{\varphi}}-\widetilde{\bm{\varphi}}\rVert_{\infty,2}^{2}\big)^{1/2}

\displaystyle=\inf_{{\bm{\varphi}}=\{{\bm{\varphi}}_{t}\}_{t\in[0,T]}\sim q,\,\widetilde{\bm{\varphi}}=\{\widetilde{\bm{\varphi}}_{t}\}_{t\in[0,T]}\sim\widetilde{q}}\Big(\mathbb{E}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\lVert{\bm{\varphi}}_{t}-\widetilde{\bm{\varphi}}_{t}\rVert_{2}^{2}\Big)^{1/2}

(C.1)

where the infimum is taken over all couplings of
q,\widetilde{q}\in\mathcal{L}_{q}
. Then
E_{q}
is complete, and hence
\mathcal{L}_{q}
is
also complete by
[
Vil09
, Theorem 6.18]
. We will write

\{q_{t}\}_{t\in[0,T]}
for the marginal laws of
q\in\mathcal{L}_{q}
.

C.1
Solution properties of the g-flow

The following result gives the solution properties of the Fisher-Rao
g
-flow
for a fixed law
q\in\mathcal{L}_{q}(T)
.

Lemma C.1
.

1.

Fix any
T>0
. Let
q\in\mathcal{L}_{q}(T)
be
such that there exists some
K<\infty
for which its marginal laws
\{q_{t}\}_{t\in[0,T]}
satisfy

\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\sup_{j\in[p]}\mathbb{E}_{{\bm{\varphi}}_{t}\sim q_{t}}e^{(8M/\tau^{2})|\varphi_{t,j}|}<K.

(C.2)

Then for any initialization
g_{0}\in\mathcal{P}_{*}(M)

that is strictly positive on
[-M,M]
,
there exists a unique solution
\{g_{t}\}_{t\in[0,T]}

to

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}g_{t}(\theta)=g_{t}(\theta)\Big(\mathcal{N}_{\tau}\ast\frac{\bar{q}_{t}}{\mathcal{N}_{\tau}\ast g_{t}}(\theta)-1\Big)

(C.3)

such that
g_{t}\in\mathcal{P}_{*}(M)
for all
t\in[0,T]
. Furthermore,

g_{t}(\theta)\geq g_{0}(\theta)e^{-t}
for all
t\in[0,T]
and
\theta\in[-M,M]
.

2.

For any
K>0
, there exists
C_{0}>0
depending only on
(M,\tau,K)

such that the following holds: Fix any
T\leq 1/C_{0}
.
For any
q,\widetilde{q}\in\mathcal{L}_{q}(T)
both satisfying the property
(
C.2
), and for any
g_{0}\in\mathcal{P}_{*}(M)
satisfying
g_{0}(\theta)>0

for all
\theta\in[-M,M]
, if
g,\widetilde{g}
are the solutions of
(
C.3
) associated to
q,\widetilde{q}
given by Part (1), then

\displaystyle d_{\infty,\mathrm{TV}}(g,\widetilde{g})\leq 2C_{0}T\cdot d_{W}(q,\widetilde{q}).

Proof of Lemma
C.1
-
Part (1)
.

Fix
g_{0}\in\mathcal{P}_{*}(M)
with
\inf_{\theta\in[-M,M]}g_{0}(\theta)>0

for all
\theta\in[-M,M]
. Define

B(g_{0})=\{g\in L^{1}([-M,M]):g(\theta)\in[g_{0}(\theta)/2,\,2g_{0}(\theta)]\text{ for Lebesgue-a.e.~}\theta\in[-M,M]\},

equipped with the
L^{1}
metric

\|g-\widetilde{g}\|_{L^{1}}=\int_{-M}^{M}|g(\theta)-\widetilde{g}(\theta)|
.
Note that
L^{1}([-M,M])
is complete, and if

g_{n}\in B(g_{0})
converges in
L^{1}
to some

g_{\ast}\in L^{1}([-M,M])
, then also
g_{n}(\theta)-g_{\ast}(\theta)\to 0
for Lebesgue-a.e.
\theta\in[-M,M]
. Then
g_{\ast}(\theta)\in[g_{0}(\theta)/2,\,2g_{0}(\theta)]
for Lebesgue-a.e.
\theta\in[-M,M]
,
implying that
B(g_{0})
is closed in
L^{1}([-M,M])
and hence complete.

For some
t_{0}>0
to be specified later, define

E(g_{0})=C([0,t_{0}],B(g_{0}))
equipped with the metric

d_{\infty,L^{1}}(g,\widetilde{g})=\sup_{t\in[0,t_{0}]}\|g_{t}-\widetilde{g}_{t}\|_{L^{1}}
.
Then
E(g_{0})
is also complete, by the above completeness of
B(g_{0})
.
Define a map
\Gamma:E(g_{0})\to E(g_{0})
with
\{g_{t}\}_{t\in[0,t_{0}]}\mapsto\{\Gamma g\}_{t\in[0,t_{0}]}
, by

\displaystyle(\Gamma g)_{t}(\theta)=g_{0}(\theta)\cdot\exp\left(\int_{0}^{t}\Big[\mathcal{N}_{\tau}\ast\frac{\bar{q}_{s}}{\mathcal{N}_{\tau}\ast g_{s}}(\theta)-1\Big]\mathrm{d}s\right).

(C.4)

We claim that for small enough
t_{0}
, this definition of
\Gamma
indeed
maps
E(g_{0})
to itself, and furthermore is contractive under
d_{\infty,L^{1}}
.
To see that
\Gamma
maps
E(g_{0})
to itself,
note that for any
g\in E(g_{0})
and
t\in[0,t_{0}]
,

\log\,(\Gamma g)_{t}(\theta)=\log g_{0}(\theta)+\int_{0}^{t}\Big[\mathcal{N}_{\tau}\ast\frac{\bar{q}_{s}}{\mathcal{N}_{\tau}\ast g_{s}}(\theta)-1\Big]\mathrm{d}s\geq\log g_{0}(\theta)-t.

(C.5)

Observe that condition (
C.2
)
implies also
\int e^{(8M/\tau^{2})|\varphi|}\bar{q}_{s}(\varphi)<K
.
Applying this,

\frac{\mathcal{N}_{\tau}(\varphi-\theta)}{\mathcal{N}_{\tau}(\varphi-\theta^{\prime})}\leq Ce^{(2M/\tau^{2})|\varphi|}
for all
\theta,\theta^{\prime}\in[-M,M]
by
Proposition
B.1
,
and
\int_{-M}^{M}g_{s}\geq\int_{-M}^{M}g_{0}/2=1/2
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}_{\tau}\ast\frac{\bar{q}_{s}}{\mathcal{N}_{\tau}\ast g_{s}}(\theta)

\displaystyle=\int\frac{\mathcal{N}_{\tau}(\varphi-\theta)}{\int_{-M}^{M}\mathcal{N}_{\tau}(\varphi-\theta^{\prime})g_{s}(\theta^{\prime})\mathrm{d}\theta^{\prime}}\bar{q}_{s}(\varphi)\mathrm{d}\varphi

\displaystyle\leq\int Ce^{(2M/\tau^{2})|\varphi|}\bar{q}_{s}(\varphi)\mathrm{d}\varphi\cdot\frac{1}{\int_{-M}^{M}g_{s}(\theta^{\prime})\mathrm{d}\theta^{\prime}}<C^{\prime}

(C.6)

for some
C^{\prime}>0
. Here and below,
C,C^{\prime}
etc. are constants depending only on

(M,\tau,K)
. So

\log\,(\Gamma g)_{t}(\theta)=\log g_{0}(\theta)+\int_{0}^{t}\Big[\mathcal{N}_{\tau}\ast\frac{\bar{q}_{s}}{\mathcal{N}_{\tau}\ast g_{s}}(\theta)-1\Big]\mathrm{d}s\leq\log g_{0}(\theta)+C^{\prime}t.

(C.7)

Choosing
t_{0}<(\log 2)/\max(C^{\prime},1)
,
this shows that for all
t\leq t_{0}
and
\theta\in[-M,M]
, we have

(\Gamma g)_{t}(\theta)\in(g_{0}(\theta)/2,\,2g_{0}(\theta))
, so
(\Gamma g)_{t}\in B(g_{0})
. Furthermore, it is clear from the definition of
\Gamma

that if
s\to t
, then

(\Gamma g)_{s}(\theta)\to(\Gamma g)_{t}(\theta)
pointwise over

\theta\in[-M,M]
. Then also
\int_{-M}^{M}|(\Gamma g)_{s}-(\Gamma g)_{t}|\to 0

by (
C.5
), (
C.7
), and the dominated convergence
theorem, so
t\mapsto(\Gamma g)_{t}
is continuous in
L^{1}
. Thus
\Gamma
is a
well-defined map from
E(g_{0})
to itself.

To see that
\Gamma
is contractive, observe that for any
g,\widetilde{g}\in E(g_{0})
and
t\in[0,t_{0}]
,

\displaystyle\int_{-M}^{M}\Big|(\Gamma g)_{t}(\theta)-(\Gamma\widetilde{g})_{t}(\theta)\Big|\mathrm{d}\theta

\displaystyle=\int_{-M}^{M}g_{0}(\theta)\Big|\exp\Big(\int_{0}^{t}\big[\mathcal{N}_{\tau}\ast\frac{\bar{q}_{s}}{\mathcal{N}_{\tau}\ast g_{s}}(\theta)-1\big]\mathrm{d}s\Big)-\exp\Big(\int_{0}^{t}\big[\mathcal{N}_{\tau}\ast\frac{\bar{q}_{s}}{\mathcal{N}_{\tau}\ast\widetilde{g}_{s}}(\theta)-1\big]\mathrm{d}s\Big)\Big|\mathrm{d}\theta

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{\theta\in[-M,M]}\Big|\exp\Big(\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{N}_{\tau}\ast\frac{\bar{q}_{s}}{\mathcal{N}_{\tau}\ast g_{s}}(\theta)\mathrm{d}s\Big)-\exp\Big(\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{N}_{\tau}\ast\frac{\bar{q}_{s}}{\mathcal{N}_{\tau}\ast\widetilde{g}_{s}}(\theta)\mathrm{d}s\Big)\Big|

\displaystyle\leq t\cdot\sup_{\theta\in[-M,M]}e^{\xi(\theta)}\cdot\sup_{s\in[0,t]}\Big|\mathcal{N}_{\tau}\ast\frac{\bar{q}_{s}}{\mathcal{N}_{\tau}\ast g_{s}}(\theta)-\mathcal{N}_{\tau}\ast\frac{\bar{q}_{s}}{\mathcal{N}_{\tau}\ast\widetilde{g}_{s}}(\theta)\Big|,

where
\xi(\theta)
is some value between
\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{N}_{\tau}\ast\frac{\bar{q}_{s}}{\mathcal{N}_{\tau}\ast g_{s}}(\theta)\mathrm{d}s
and
\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{N}_{\tau}\ast\frac{\bar{q}_{s}}{\mathcal{N}_{\tau}\ast\widetilde{g}_{s}}(\theta)\mathrm{d}s
. Using
(
C.6
), we have
\sup_{\theta\in[-M,M]}e^{\xi(\theta)}<2

because
t\leq t_{0}<(\log 2)/C^{\prime}
.
Moreover, using again Proposition
B.1
and
the lower bounds
\int_{-M}^{M}g_{s}(\theta)\geq 1/2
and

\int_{-M}^{M}\widetilde{g}_{s}(\theta)\geq 1/2
for all

s\in[0,t_{0}]
, we have

\displaystyle\sup_{\theta\in[-M,M]}\sup_{s\in[0,t]}\Big|\mathcal{N}_{\tau}\ast\frac{\bar{q}_{s}}{\mathcal{N}_{\tau}\ast g_{s}}(\theta)-\mathcal{N}_{\tau}\ast\frac{\bar{q}_{s}}{\mathcal{N}_{\tau}\ast\widetilde{g}_{s}}(\theta)\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{\theta\in[-M,M]}\sup_{s\in[0,t]}\int_{-M}^{M}\int\frac{\mathcal{N}_{\tau}(\varphi-\theta)\mathcal{N}_{\tau}(\varphi-\theta^{\prime})\bar{q}_{s}(\varphi)}{\int_{-M}^{M}\mathcal{N}_{\tau}(\varphi-\theta^{\prime})g_{s}(\theta^{\prime})\mathrm{d}\theta^{\prime}\int_{-M}^{M}\mathcal{N}_{\tau}(\varphi-\theta^{\prime})\widetilde{g}_{s}(\theta^{\prime})\mathrm{d}\theta^{\prime}}\cdot|g_{s}(\theta^{\prime})-\widetilde{g}_{s}(\theta^{\prime})|\mathrm{d}\varphi\mathrm{d}\theta^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{s\in[0,t]}\int Ce^{(4M/\tau^{2})|\varphi|}\bar{q}_{s}(\varphi)\mathrm{d}\varphi\cdot\sup_{s\in[0,t]}\frac{1}{\int_{-M}^{M}g_{s}(\theta^{\prime})\mathrm{d}\theta^{\prime}\cdot\int_{-M}^{M}\widetilde{g}_{s}(\theta^{\prime})\mathrm{d}\theta^{\prime}}\cdot\sup_{s\in[0,t]}\int_{-M}^{M}|g_{s}(\theta^{\prime})-\widetilde{g}_{s}(\theta^{\prime})|\mathrm{d}\theta^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq C^{\prime\prime}\lVert g-\widetilde{g}\rVert_{\infty,L^{1}}.

For some
C^{\prime\prime}>0
. In summary,

\displaystyle\lVert\Gamma g-\Gamma\widetilde{g}\rVert_{\infty,L^{1}}=\sup_{t\in[0,t_{0}]}\int_{-M}^{M}\Big|(\Gamma g)_{t}(\theta)-(\Gamma\widetilde{g})_{t}(\theta)\Big|\mathrm{d}\theta\leq 2C^{\prime\prime}t_{0}\lVert g-\widetilde{g}\rVert_{\infty,L^{1}}.

Choosing
t_{0}\leq 1/4C^{\prime\prime}
, this
establishes that
\Gamma:E(g_{0})\rightarrow E(g_{0})
is a
contraction.

By the Banach fixed point theorem, there exists a unique fixed
point
g=\{g_{t}\}_{t\in[0,t_{0}]}\in E(g_{0})
of
\Gamma
. Choosing the version
of
g
satisfying (
C.4
) with pointwise equality for all
t\in[0,t_{0}]
and
\theta\in[-M,M]
, we must then have

\log g_{t}(\theta)=\log g_{0}(\theta)+\int_{0}^{t}\Big[\mathcal{N}_{\tau}\ast\frac{\bar{q}_{s}}{\mathcal{N}_{\tau}\ast g_{s}}(\theta)-1\Big]\mathrm{d}s

(C.8)

for all
t\in[0,t_{0}]
and
\theta\in[-M,M]
.
The functions
{\bm{\varphi}}\mapsto p^{-1}\sum_{j=1}^{p}\frac{\mathcal{N}_{\tau}(\varphi_{j}-\theta)}{\mathcal{N}_{\tau}*g_{s}(\varphi_{j})}q_{s}({\bm{\varphi}})

are uniformly integrable over
s\in[0,t_{0}]
, by
Proposition
B.1
and the condition (
C.2
)
for
q_{s}
. Thus
s\mapsto\mathcal{N}_{\tau}*\frac{\bar{q}_{s}}{\mathcal{N}_{\tau}*g_{s}}(\theta)=p^{-1}\sum_{j=1}^{p}\int\frac{\mathcal{N}_{\tau}(\varphi_{j}-\theta)}{\mathcal{N}_{\tau}*g_{s}(\varphi_{j})}q_{s}({\bm{\varphi}})\mathrm{d}{\bm{\varphi}}

is continuous in
s
, so
g_{t}(\theta)
is
C^{1}
in
t
. Differentiating
(
C.8
) in
t
shows that
\{g_{t}\}_{t\in[0,t_{0}]}
solves
(
C.3
). Furthermore,
g_{t}\in\mathcal{P}_{*}(M)
for all
t\in[0,t_{0}]
,
because

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\int_{-M}^{M}g_{t}=\int_{-M}^{M}g_{t}\cdot\partial_{t}\log g_{t}=\int_{-M}^{M}g_{t}\cdot\Big[\mathcal{N}_{\tau}\ast\frac{\bar{q}_{t}}{\mathcal{N}_{\tau}\ast g_{t}}-1\Big]=1-\int_{-M}^{M}g_{t},

which has the unique solution
\int_{-M}^{M}g_{t}=1
with initialization

\int_{-M}^{M}g_{0}=1
.

Conversely, let
\{\widetilde{g}_{t}\}_{t\in[0,t_{0}]}
be any other
solution of (
C.3
) where
\widetilde{g}_{t}\in\mathcal{P}_{*}(M)
for all
t\in[0,t_{0}]
. Suppose by contradiction that there exists some

\theta\in[-M,M]
for which
\widetilde{g}_{t}(\theta)\notin[g_{0}(\theta)/2,\,2g_{0}(\theta)]
for some
t\in[0,t_{0}]
. Fixing this
\theta
,
let
t^{\prime}
be the infimum of all such
t
. Then

\widetilde{g}_{t^{\prime}}(\theta)=g_{0}(\theta)/2
or
\widetilde{g}_{t^{\prime}}=2g_{0}(\theta)
by
continuity of
t\mapsto\widetilde{g}_{t}(\theta)
. On the other hand, we must have

\log\widetilde{g}_{t}(\theta)\geq\log g_{0}(\theta)-t
and

\log\widetilde{g}_{t}(\theta)\leq\log g_{0}(\theta)+C^{\prime}t
for all
t\in[0,t^{\prime}]
,
by the same arguments as (
C.5
) and (
C.7
)
where we may use
\int_{-M}^{M}\widetilde{g}_{s}=1
in place of the previously used
condition
\int_{-M}^{M}g_{s}\geq 1/2
to derive (
C.7
).
Since
t^{\prime}\leq t_{0}<(\log 2)/\max(C^{\prime},1)
, integrating these inequalities leads to
the contradiction that

\widetilde{g}_{t^{\prime}}(\theta)>g_{0}(\theta)/2
and
\widetilde{g}_{t^{\prime}}(\theta)<2g_{0}(\theta)

strictly. Thus,
\widetilde{g}_{t}(\theta)\in[g_{0}(\theta)/2,\,2g_{0}(\theta)]
for all

t\in[0,t_{0}]
and
\theta\in[-M,M]
. So

\{\widetilde{g}_{t}\}_{t\in[0,t_{0}]}
is also a fixed point of
\Gamma
in
E(g_{0})
,
implying by uniqueness of this fixed point that
g_{t}=\widetilde{g}_{t}

as distributions in
\mathcal{P}(M)
for all
t\in[0,t_{0}]
. This implies that
the right sides of (
C.8
) are equal for

g_{t}
and
\widetilde{g}_{t}
, and consequently the left sides are equal pointwise,
i.e.
g_{t}(\theta)=\widetilde{g}_{t}(\theta)
pointwise for all
\theta\in[-M,M]

and
t\in[0,t_{0}]
. Thus the solution

\{g_{t}\}_{t\in[0,t_{0}]}
to (
C.3
) is unique.

This solution satisfies that
g_{t_{0}}
is a strictly positive density in

\mathcal{P}_{*}(M)
. Then we may
apply the above argument sequentially to
[t_{0},2t_{0}]
,
[2t_{0},3t_{0}]
etc. with

g_{t_{0}}
,
g_{2t_{0}}
etc. in place of
g_{0}
. This proves the existence and
uniqueness of the solution
\{g_{t}\}_{t\in[0,T]}
to (
C.3
).
Finally, from (
C.8
) we get
g_{t}(\theta)/g_{0}(\theta)\geq e^{-t}
for all
\theta\in[-M,M]
and
t\in[0,T]
,
completing the proof of Part (1).
∎

Proof of Lemma
C.1
-
Part (2)
.

By definition,

\displaystyle g_{t}(\theta)

\displaystyle=g_{0}(\theta)+\int_{0}^{t}g_{s}(\theta)\Big[\mathcal{N}_{\tau}\ast\frac{\bar{q}_{s}}{\mathcal{N}_{\tau}\ast g_{s}}(\theta)-1\Big]\mathrm{d}s,

\displaystyle\widetilde{g}_{t}(\theta)

\displaystyle=g_{0}(\theta)+\int_{0}^{t}\widetilde{g}_{s}(\theta)\Big[\mathcal{N}_{\tau}\ast\frac{\bar{\widetilde{q}}_{s}}{\mathcal{N}_{\tau}\ast g_{s}}(\theta)-1\Big]\mathrm{d}s.

Then for any
t\in[0,T]
,

\displaystyle\int_{-M}^{M}|g_{t}(\theta)-\widetilde{g}_{t}(\theta)|\mathrm{d}\theta

\displaystyle\leq\int_{-M}^{M}\Big|\int_{0}^{t}\bigg(g_{s}(\theta)\Big[\mathcal{N}_{\tau}\ast\frac{\bar{q}_{s}}{\mathcal{N}_{\tau}\ast g_{s}}(\theta)\Big]-\widetilde{g}_{s}(\theta)\Big[\mathcal{N}_{\tau}\ast\frac{\bar{\widetilde{q}}_{s}}{\mathcal{N}_{\tau}\ast\widetilde{g}_{s}}(\theta)\Big]\bigg)\mathrm{d}s\Big|\mathrm{d}\theta

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{-M}^{M}|g_{s}(\theta)-\widetilde{g}_{s}(\theta)|\mathrm{d}\theta\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\leq(I)+(II)+(III)+2T\cdot d_{\mathrm{TV}}(g,\widetilde{g}),

(C.9)

where

\displaystyle(I)

\displaystyle=\int_{-M}^{M}\int_{0}^{t}\big|g_{s}(\theta)-\widetilde{g}_{s}(\theta)\big|\cdot\mathcal{N}_{\tau}\ast\frac{\bar{q}_{s}}{\mathcal{N}_{\tau}\ast g_{s}}(\theta)\mathrm{d}s\mathrm{d}\theta,

\displaystyle(II)

\displaystyle=\int_{-M}^{M}\int_{0}^{t}\widetilde{g}_{s}(\theta)\cdot\Big|\mathcal{N}_{\tau}\ast\frac{\bar{q}_{s}-\bar{\widetilde{q}}_{s}}{\mathcal{N}_{\tau}\ast g_{s}}(\theta)\Big|\mathrm{d}s\mathrm{d}\theta,

\displaystyle(III)

\displaystyle=\int_{-M}^{M}\int_{0}^{t}\widetilde{g}_{s}(\theta)\cdot\Big|\mathcal{N}_{\tau}\ast\Big(\frac{\bar{\widetilde{q}}_{s}}{\mathcal{N}_{\tau}\ast g_{s}}-\frac{\bar{\widetilde{q}}_{s}}{\mathcal{N}_{\tau}\ast\widetilde{g}_{s}}\Big)(\theta)\Big|\mathrm{d}s\mathrm{d}\theta.

Let
C,C^{\prime}
etc. denote constants depending only on
(M,\tau,K)
and changing
from instance to instance.
Applying Proposition
B.1
, we have
\mathcal{N}_{\tau}\ast\frac{\bar{q}_{s}}{\mathcal{N}_{\tau}\ast g_{s}}(\theta)\leq C\int e^{(2M/\tau^{2})|\varphi|}\bar{q}_{s}(\varphi)\mathrm{d}\varphi
for any
\theta\in[-M,M]
, hence

\displaystyle(I)\leq Ct\sup_{s\in[0,t]}\Big(\int_{-M}^{M}\big|g_{s}(\theta)-\widetilde{g}_{s}(\theta)\big|\mathrm{d}\theta\cdot\int e^{(2M/\tau^{2})|\varphi|}\bar{q}_{s}(\varphi)\mathrm{d}\varphi\Big)\leq C^{\prime}T\cdot d_{\infty,\mathrm{TV}}(g,\widetilde{g}).

Similarly,

\displaystyle(III)

\displaystyle\leq\int_{-M}^{M}\int_{0}^{t}\widetilde{g}_{s}(\theta)\cdot\mathcal{N}_{\tau}(\theta-\varphi)\bar{\widetilde{q}}_{s}(\varphi)\cdot\frac{\int_{-M}^{M}\mathcal{N}_{\tau}(\varphi-\theta^{\prime})|g_{s}(\theta^{\prime})-\widetilde{g}_{s}(\theta^{\prime})|\mathrm{d}\theta^{\prime}}{\mathcal{N}_{\tau}*g_{s}(\varphi)\cdot\mathcal{N}_{\tau}*\widetilde{g}_{s}(\varphi)}\mathrm{d}s\mathrm{d}\theta

\displaystyle\leq Ct\sup_{s\in[0,t]}\Big(\int_{-M}^{M}\big|g_{s}(\theta^{\prime})-\widetilde{g}_{s}(\theta^{\prime})\big|\mathrm{d}\theta^{\prime}\cdot\int e^{(4M/\tau^{2})|\varphi|}\bar{\widetilde{q}}_{s}(\varphi)\mathrm{d}\varphi\Big)\leq C^{\prime}T\cdot d_{\infty,\mathrm{TV}}(g,\widetilde{g}).

To bound
(II)
, let
{\bm{\varphi}}=\{{\bm{\varphi}}_{t}\}_{t\in[0,T]}
and

\widetilde{\bm{\varphi}}=\{\widetilde{\bm{\varphi}}_{t}\}_{t\in[0,T]}
be two processes on the same probability space
such that
{\bm{\varphi}}\sim q
and
\widetilde{\bm{\varphi}}\sim\widetilde{q}
. Then

\displaystyle(II)

\displaystyle=\int_{-M}^{M}\int_{0}^{t}\widetilde{g}_{s}(\theta)\cdot\Big|\int\frac{\mathcal{N}_{\tau}(\theta-\varphi)}{\mathcal{N}_{\tau}\ast g_{s}(\varphi)}\big(\bar{q}_{s}(\varphi)-\bar{\widetilde{q}}_{s}(\varphi)\big)\mathrm{d}\varphi\Big|\mathrm{d}s\mathrm{d}\theta

\displaystyle=\int_{-M}^{M}\int_{0}^{t}\widetilde{g}_{s}(\theta)\cdot\big|\frac{1}{p}\sum_{j=1}^{p}\mathbb{E}\mathsf{f}_{s}(\varphi_{s,j};\theta)-\mathbb{E}\mathsf{f}_{s}(\widetilde{\varphi}_{s,j};\theta)\big|\mathrm{d}s\mathrm{d}\theta,

where
\mathsf{f}_{s}(\varphi;\theta)=\frac{\mathcal{N}_{\tau}(\varphi-\theta)}{\mathcal{N}_{\tau}\ast g_{s}(\varphi)}
. For any

\theta\in[-M,M]
, applying Proposition
B.1
,

\displaystyle\big|\mathsf{f}^{\prime}_{s}(\varphi;\theta)\big|=\frac{1}{\tau^{2}}\Big|\frac{\mathcal{N}_{\tau}(\varphi-\theta)\cdot\theta}{\mathcal{N}_{\tau}\ast g_{s}(\varphi)}-\frac{\mathcal{N}_{\tau}(\varphi-\theta)\cdot\int\mathcal{N}_{\tau}(\varphi-\theta^{\prime})\cdot\theta^{\prime}g_{s}(\theta^{\prime})\mathrm{d}\theta^{\prime}}{\big(\mathcal{N}_{\tau}\ast g_{s}(\varphi)\big)^{2}}\Big|\leq Ce^{(4M/\tau^{2})|\varphi|},

so it holds that

\displaystyle\big|\mathbb{E}\mathsf{f}_{s}(\varphi_{s,j};\theta)-\mathbb{E}\mathsf{f}_{s}(\widetilde{\varphi}_{s,j};\theta)\big|

\displaystyle\leq C\,\mathbb{E}\big(e^{(4M/\tau^{2})|\varphi_{s,j}|}+e^{(4M/\tau^{2})|\widetilde{\varphi}_{s,j}|}\big)\cdot|\varphi_{s,j}-\widetilde{\varphi}_{s,j}|

\displaystyle\leq C\big(\mathbb{E}e^{(8M/\tau^{2})|\varphi_{s,j}|}+e^{(8M/\tau^{2})|\widetilde{\varphi}_{s,j}|}\big)^{1/2}\cdot\big(\mathbb{E}(\varphi_{s,j}-\widetilde{\varphi}_{s,j})^{2}\big)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq C^{\prime}\big(\mathbb{E}(\varphi_{s,j}-\widetilde{\varphi}_{s,j})^{2}\big)^{1/2},

using the condition (
C.2
) for
\{q_{t}\}
and

\{\widetilde{q}_{t}\}
. Therefore,

\displaystyle(II)\leq C\int_{0}^{t}\frac{1}{p}\sum_{j=1}^{p}\big[\mathbb{E}(\varphi_{s,j}-\widetilde{\varphi}_{s,j})^{2}\big]^{1/2}\mathrm{d}s\leq C^{\prime}T\cdot\big(\mathbb{E}\sup_{s\in[0,T]}\lVert{\bm{\varphi}}_{s}-\widetilde{{\bm{\varphi}}}_{s}\rVert_{2}^{2}\big)^{1/2}.

Taking the infimum over couplings of
q,\widetilde{q}\in\mathcal{L}_{q}
yields

(II)\leq C^{\prime}T\cdot d_{W}(q,\widetilde{q})
.
Plugging the bounds for
(I),(II),(III)
into (
C.1
)
yields, for some
C_{0}>0
(not depending on
T
),

\displaystyle d_{\infty,\mathrm{TV}}(g,\widetilde{g})\leq C_{0}T\cdot(d_{\infty,\mathrm{TV}}(g,\widetilde{g})+d_{W}(q,\widetilde{q}))

Then choosing
T\leq 1/(2C_{0})
and rearranging concludes the proof.
∎

An immediate corollary is the existence and uniqueness of the univariate g-flow

\{g_{t}\}_{t\geq 0}
in the setting of Theorem
3.3
.

Corollary C.2
.

Fix any
T>0
.
Let
\bar{q}\in\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{R})
satisfy
\int e^{\lambda\varphi}\bar{q}(\varphi)\mathrm{d}\varphi<\infty
for all
\lambda\in\mathbb{R}
. Then, given any initial
density
g_{0}\in\mathcal{P}_{*}(M)
that is strictly positive on
[-M,M]
,
there exists a unique solution
\{g_{t}\}_{t\in[0,T]}
to (
3.2
)
where
g_{t}\in\mathcal{P}_{*}(M)
for all
t\in[0,T]
. Furthermore,

g_{t}(\theta)\geq g_{0}(\theta)e^{-t}
for all
\theta\in[-M,M]
and
t\in[0,T]
.

Proof.

Let
q\in\mathcal{L}_{q}
be the law of a constant process

{\bm{\varphi}}_{t}={\bm{\varphi}}_{0}
for all
t\in[0,T]
, where coordinates of
{\bm{\varphi}}_{0}
are
i.i.d. with distribution
\bar{q}
. Then the condition (
C.2
) for
q

is satisfied by the given moment generating function condition for
\bar{q}
, and

\bar{q}_{t}=\bar{q}
for all
t\in[0,T]
, so the result follows from
Lemma
C.1
-(1).
∎

C.2
Solution properties of the q-flow

Recall the negative log-posterior-density
U_{g}(\cdot)
of
{\bm{\varphi}}

from (
3.5
).
The following result gives the solution properties of the Wasserstein
q
-flow
for any fixed
g=\{g_{t}\}_{t\in[0,T]}\in\mathcal{L}_{g}(T)
.

Lemma C.3
.

1.

Fix any
T>0
, and let
g=\{g_{t}\}_{t\in[0,T]}\in\mathcal{L}_{g}(T)
.
Then for any initialization
q_{0}\in\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{R}^{p})
,
there is a strong solution
{\bm{\varphi}}=\{{\bm{\varphi}}_{t}\}_{t\in[0,T]}
to the Langevin diffusion

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}{\bm{\varphi}}_{t}=-\nabla U_{g_{t}}({\bm{\varphi}}_{t})\mathrm{d}t+\sqrt{2}\,\mathrm{d}\mathbf{B}_{t},

(C.10)

which is pathwise unique given the initialization
{\bm{\varphi}}_{0}\sim q_{0}
.

The marginal laws

\{q_{t}\}_{t\in[0,T]}
of
\{{\bm{\varphi}}_{t}\}_{t\in[0,T]}

form the unique weak solution valued in
\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{R}^{p})
to the Fokker-Planck PDE

\partial_{t}q_{t}({\bm{\varphi}})=\nabla\cdot[q_{t}({\bm{\varphi}})\nabla U_{g_{t}}({\bm{\varphi}})]+\Delta q_{t}({\bm{\varphi}}),

(C.11)

in the sense that
q_{t}\Rightarrow q_{0}
weakly as
t\to 0
and

0=\int_{0}^{t}\int\Big(\partial_{s}\zeta_{s}({\bm{\varphi}})-\nabla U_{g_{s}}({\bm{\varphi}})^{\top}\nabla\zeta_{s}({\bm{\varphi}})+\Delta\zeta_{s}({\bm{\varphi}})\Big)q_{s}(\mathrm{d}{\bm{\varphi}})\mathrm{d}s

(C.12)

for any smooth function
(t,\varphi)\mapsto\zeta_{t}({\bm{\varphi}})
with compact
support in
(0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{p}
.

2.

Let
q_{0}\in\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{R}^{p})
satisfy

\mathbb{E}_{{\bm{\varphi}}_{0}\sim q_{0}}e^{{\bm{\lambda}}^{\top}{\bm{\varphi}}_{0}}<\infty

for all
{\bm{\lambda}}\in\mathbb{R}^{p}
. Then, for each
{\bm{\lambda}}\in\mathbb{R}^{p}
,
there exists some
C(q_{0},{\bm{\lambda}})<\infty
(depending also on

n,p,\mathbf{X},\mathbf{y},\sigma,\tau,M
) such that for any
T>0
and
g\in\mathcal{L}_{g}(T)
,
the law
q\in\mathcal{L}_{q}(T)
of the solution
\{{\bm{\varphi}}_{t}\}_{t\in[0,T]}

of Part (1) satisfies

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\mathbb{E}_{{\bm{\varphi}}_{t}\sim q_{t}}e^{{\bm{\lambda}}^{\top}{\bm{\varphi}}_{t}}<C(q_{0},{\bm{\lambda}}).

(C.13)

In particular, there exists some
K(q_{0})<\infty
such that for any
T>0

and
g\in\mathcal{L}_{g}(T)
,

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\max_{j\in[p]}\mathbb{E}_{{\bm{\varphi}}_{t}\sim q_{t}}e^{(8M/\tau^{2})|\varphi_{t,j}|}\leq K(q_{0}).

(C.14)

3.

For any
K>0
, there exist
T_{0},C_{0}>0
(depending on
K
and also

(n,p,\mathbf{X},\mathbf{y},\sigma,\tau,M)
)
such that the following holds: Let
q_{0}\in\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{R}^{p})

be any initialization as in Part (2), such that
(
C.14
) holds with
K(q_{0})=K
.
Then for any
T\leq T_{0}
and
g,\widetilde{g}\in\mathcal{L}_{g}(T)
, if

q,\widetilde{q}\in\mathcal{L}_{q}(T)
are the associated solutions of
(
C.10
) given by Part (1), then

\displaystyle d_{W}(q,\widetilde{q})\leq C_{0}T\cdot d_{\infty,\mathrm{TV}}(g,\widetilde{g}).

Proof of Lemma
C.3
-
Part (1)
.

We show that
\nabla U_{g}:\mathbb{R}^{p}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{p}
is Lipschitz uniformly
over
g\in\mathcal{P}(M)
. Observe that by Tweedie’s formula,

\displaystyle\nabla U_{g}({\bm{\varphi}})

\displaystyle=-\mathbf{X}^{\top}{\bm{\Sigma}}^{-1}(\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{X}{\bm{\varphi}})-\left(\frac{[\mathcal{N}_{\tau}\ast g]^{\prime}(\varphi_{j})}{[\mathcal{N}_{\tau}\ast g](\varphi_{j})}\right)_{j=1}^{p}

(C.15)

\displaystyle=\big(\mathbf{X}^{\top}{\bm{\Sigma}}^{-1}\mathbf{X}+\tau^{-2}\operatorname{Id}\big){\bm{\varphi}}-\mathbf{X}^{\top}{\bm{\Sigma}}^{-1}\mathbf{y}-\tau^{-2}\theta_{g}({\bm{\varphi}})

(C.16)

where
\theta_{g}(\varphi)=\mathbb{E}_{g}[\theta\mid\varphi]
is the posterior mean in the
scalar model

\varphi=\theta+z,\qquad\theta\sim g,\qquad z\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\tau^{2}),

and
\theta_{g}({\bm{\varphi}})
is its entrywise application to

{\bm{\varphi}}=(\varphi_{1},\ldots,\varphi_{p})
. We have

|\theta_{g}^{\prime}(\varphi)|=|\tau^{-2}\operatorname{Var}[\theta\mid\varphi]|\leq M^{2}/\tau^{2}
.
Then
\lVert\nabla U_{g}({\bm{\varphi}})-\nabla U_{g}(\widetilde{\bm{\varphi}})\rVert_{2}\leq C\lVert{\bm{\varphi}}-\widetilde{\bm{\varphi}}\rVert_{2}
for some
C>0
(depending on

\mathbf{X},\mathbf{y},\sigma,\tau,M
), uniformly over
g\in\mathcal{P}(M)
, as claimed.

From the weak continuity of
t\mapsto g_{t}
and the form
(
C.15
) for
U_{g}
, we see that
(t,{\bm{\varphi}})\mapsto U_{g_{t}}({\bm{\varphi}})
is jointly continuous.
Then the existence of a pathwise unique strong
solution
{\bm{\varphi}}=\{{\bm{\varphi}}_{t}\}_{t\in[0,T]}

to the Langevin diffusion (
C.10
) follows from

[
LG16
, Theorem 8.3]
. By Ito’s formula, for any smooth bounded
function
\zeta_{t}({\bm{\varphi}})
, we have

\mathbb{E}[\zeta_{t}({\bm{\varphi}}_{t})-\zeta_{0}({\bm{\varphi}}_{0})]=\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{0}^{t}\Big(\partial_{s}\zeta_{s}({\bm{\varphi}}_{s})-\nabla U_{g_{s}}({\bm{\varphi}}_{s})^{\top}\nabla\zeta_{t}({\bm{\varphi}}_{s})+\Delta\zeta_{s}({\bm{\varphi}}_{s})\Big)\mathrm{d}s\right]

(C.17)

and the statement (
C.12
) follows from
applying the boundary condition
\zeta_{t}({\bm{\varphi}}_{t})=\zeta_{0}({\bm{\varphi}}_{0})=0
.
This is the unique weak solution to
(
C.11
) for which every
q_{t}\in\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{R}^{p})
,
by
[
BRS11
, Theorem 4.9]
.
∎

Proof of Lemma
C.3
-
Part (2)
.

Let
\{{\bm{\varphi}}_{t}\}_{t\in[0,T]}
be a solution of the Langevin diffusion
in Part (1), with initialization
{\bm{\varphi}}_{0}\sim q_{0}
.
Let
{\bm{\Gamma}}=\mathbf{X}^{\top}{\bm{\Sigma}}^{-1}\mathbf{X}+\tau^{-2}\operatorname{Id}\in\mathbb{R}^{p\times p}
, and
let
\mathbf{u}\in\mathbb{R}^{p}
be an eigenvector of
{\bm{\Gamma}}
(having arbitrary
\ell_{2}
norm) with eigenvalue

\mu>0
.
Fix some
B>0
to be specified later. Then

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}e^{\mathbf{u}^{\top}{\bm{\varphi}}_{t}}=\mathbb{E}e^{\mathbf{u}^{\top}{\bm{\varphi}}_{t}}\bm{1}_{\mathbf{u}^{\top}{\bm{\varphi}}_{t}\leq B}+\mathbb{E}e^{\mathbf{u}^{\top}{\bm{\varphi}}_{t}}\bm{1}_{\mathbf{u}^{\top}{\bm{\varphi}}_{t}>B}\leq e^{B}+\mathbb{E}e^{\mathbf{u}^{\top}{\bm{\varphi}}_{t}}\bm{1}_{\mathbf{u}^{\top}{\bm{\varphi}}_{t}>B}.

To bound the second term, let
h
be a twice
differentiable function such that: (i)
h(u)=0
for
u\leq B-1
; (ii)

h(u)=1
for
u\geq B
; (iii)
h(u),h^{\prime}(u)\geq 0
for
u\in\mathbb{R}
; (iv)

\sup_{u\in\mathbb{R}}\max(h^{\prime}(u),|h^{\prime\prime}(u)|)<C
for some
C>0
.
Then with
f({\bm{\varphi}})=e^{\mathbf{u}^{\top}{\bm{\varphi}}}h(\mathbf{u}^{\top}{\bm{\varphi}})
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}e^{\mathbf{u}^{\top}{\bm{\varphi}}_{t}}\bm{1}_{\mathbf{u}^{\top}{\bm{\varphi}}_{t}>B}\leq\mathbb{E}e^{\mathbf{u}^{\top}{\bm{\varphi}}_{t}}h(\mathbf{u}^{\top}{\bm{\varphi}}_{t})=\mathbb{E}f({\bm{\varphi}}_{t}).

Using Ito’s formula, we have

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\mathbb{E}f({\bm{\varphi}}_{t})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left\langle\nabla f({\bm{\varphi}}_{t}),\mathrm{d}{\bm{\varphi}}_{t}\right\rangle+\mathbb{E}\operatorname{Tr}(\nabla^{2}f({\bm{\varphi}}_{t}))

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left\langle\nabla f({\bm{\varphi}}_{t}),-\nabla U_{g_{t}}({\bm{\varphi}}_{t})\right\rangle+\mathbb{E}\operatorname{Tr}(\nabla^{2}f({\bm{\varphi}}_{t})).

(C.18)

For the first term, note from (
C.16
) that
-\nabla U_{g_{t}}({\bm{\varphi}})=-{\bm{\Gamma}}{\bm{\varphi}}+\mathbf{s}
where

\mathbf{s}=\mathbf{X}^{\top}{\bm{\Sigma}}^{-1}\mathbf{y}+\tau^{-2}\theta_{g_{t}}({\bm{\varphi}})

Since
g_{t}
is supported on
[-M,M]
, there exists some
C>0

(depending on
\mathbf{X},\mathbf{y},\sigma,\tau,M
)
such that
\lVert\mathbf{s}\rVert<C
. Hence using

\displaystyle\nabla f({\bm{\varphi}})=\mathbf{u}e^{\mathbf{u}^{\top}{\bm{\varphi}}}h(\mathbf{u}^{\top}{\bm{\varphi}})+\mathbf{u}e^{\mathbf{u}^{\top}{\bm{\varphi}}}h^{\prime}(\mathbf{u}^{\top}{\bm{\varphi}})=\mathbf{u}\cdot\big(f({\bm{\varphi}})+e^{\mathbf{u}^{\top}{\bm{\varphi}}}h^{\prime}(\mathbf{u}^{\top}{\bm{\varphi}})\big),

and applying
{\bm{\Gamma}}\mathbf{u}=\mu\cdot\mathbf{u}
,
f(\cdot)\geq 0
, and
h^{\prime}(\cdot)\geq 0
,
we have

\displaystyle\left\langle\nabla f({\bm{\varphi}}),-\nabla U_{g_{t}}({\bm{\varphi}})\right\rangle

\displaystyle=-\big(f({\bm{\varphi}})+e^{\mathbf{u}^{\top}{\bm{\varphi}}}h^{\prime}(\mathbf{u}^{\top}{\bm{\varphi}})\big)\cdot\mathbf{u}^{\top}{\bm{\Gamma}}{\bm{\varphi}}+\big(f({\bm{\varphi}})+e^{\mathbf{u}^{\top}{\bm{\varphi}}}h^{\prime}(\mathbf{u}^{\top}{\bm{\varphi}})\big)\mathbf{u}^{\top}\mathbf{s}

\displaystyle\leq-(\mu\cdot\mathbf{u}^{\top}{\bm{\varphi}}-\lVert\mathbf{u}\rVert\lVert\mathbf{s}\rVert)f({\bm{\varphi}})-\mu e^{\mathbf{u}^{\top}{\bm{\varphi}}}h^{\prime}(\mathbf{u}^{\top}{\bm{\varphi}})\cdot\mathbf{u}^{\top}{\bm{\varphi}}+\lVert\mathbf{u}\rVert\lVert\mathbf{s}\rVert\cdot e^{\mathbf{u}^{\top}{\bm{\varphi}}}h^{\prime}(\mathbf{u}^{\top}{\bm{\varphi}}).

Choose
B=B(\lVert\mathbf{u}\rVert,\mu)>1
large enough such that

\mu\cdot\mathbf{u}^{\top}{\bm{\varphi}}-\|\mathbf{u}\|\,\|\mathbf{s}\|>B\mu/2
whenever
{\bm{\varphi}}\in\mathbb{R}^{p}
satisfies

\mathbf{u}^{\top}{\bm{\varphi}}>B-1
. Then, the first term is bounded above by

-B\mu/2\cdot f({\bm{\varphi}})
(which holds also when
\mathbf{u}^{\top}{\bm{\varphi}}\leq B-1
, in which
case
f({\bm{\varphi}})=0
). The second term
-\mu e^{\mathbf{u}^{\top}{\bm{\varphi}}}h^{\prime}(\mathbf{u}^{\top}{\bm{\varphi}})\cdot\mathbf{u}^{\top}{\bm{\varphi}}
is non-positive, because
h^{\prime}(\mathbf{u}^{\top}{\bm{\varphi}})\geq 0
and equals 0 unless
\mathbf{u}^{\top}{\bm{\varphi}}>B-1>0
.
For the third term, since also
h^{\prime}(\mathbf{u}^{\top}{\bm{\varphi}})=0
unless
\mathbf{u}^{\top}{\bm{\varphi}}\leq B
, it is
bounded by some
C=C(\lVert\mathbf{u}\rVert,\mu)>0
. In summary,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left\langle\nabla f({\bm{\varphi}}_{t}),-\nabla U_{g_{t}}({\bm{\varphi}}_{t})\right\rangle\leq-\frac{B\mu}{2}\,\mathbb{E}f({\bm{\varphi}}_{t})+C.

On the other hand, since

\displaystyle\nabla^{2}f({\bm{\varphi}})=\mathbf{u}\mathbf{u}^{\top}\Big(e^{\mathbf{u}^{\top}{\bm{\varphi}}}h(\mathbf{u}^{\top}{\bm{\varphi}})+2e^{\mathbf{u}^{\top}{\bm{\varphi}}}h^{\prime}(\mathbf{u}^{\top}{\bm{\varphi}})+e^{\mathbf{u}^{\top}{\bm{\varphi}}}h^{\prime\prime}(\mathbf{u}^{\top}{\bm{\varphi}})\Big),

we have

\displaystyle\tr\big(\nabla^{2}f({\bm{\varphi}})\big)=\lVert\mathbf{u}\rVert^{2}\cdot f({\bm{\varphi}})+\lVert\mathbf{u}\rVert^{2}\Big(2e^{\mathbf{u}^{\top}{\bm{\varphi}}}h^{\prime}(\mathbf{u}^{\top}{\bm{\varphi}})+e^{\mathbf{u}^{\top}{\bm{\varphi}}}h^{\prime\prime}(\mathbf{u}^{\top}{\bm{\varphi}})\Big)

Using
h^{\prime}(\mathbf{u}^{\top}{\bm{\varphi}})=h^{\prime\prime}(\mathbf{u}^{\top}{\bm{\varphi}})=0
unless
\mathbf{u}^{\top}{\bm{\varphi}}\leq B
, and choosing

B=B(\|\mathbf{u}\|,\mu)
large enough so that
B\mu\geq 4\lVert\mathbf{u}\rVert^{2}
, for some

C=C(\|\mathbf{u}\|,\mu)>0
we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\tr(\nabla^{2}f({\bm{\varphi}}_{t}))\leq\frac{B\mu}{4}\mathbb{E}f({\bm{\varphi}}_{t})+C.

Plugging the above bounds into (
C.2
), we have

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\mathbb{E}f({\bm{\varphi}}_{t})\leq-\frac{B\mu}{4}\mathbb{E}f({\bm{\varphi}}_{t})+C^{\prime}.

This implies that
\mathbb{E}f({\bm{\varphi}}_{t})\leq x(t)
where

x(t)=(\mathbb{E}[f({\bm{\varphi}}_{0})]+4C^{\prime}/B\mu)e^{-tB\mu/4}-4C^{\prime}/B\mu
is the solution of

x^{\prime}(t)=-(B\mu/4)x(t)+C^{\prime}
with initialization
x(0)=\mathbb{E}f({\bm{\varphi}}_{0})
.
If
\mathbb{E}e^{\mathbf{u}^{\top}{\bm{\varphi}}_{0}}<A
, then also

\mathbb{E}f({\bm{\varphi}}_{0})\leq\mathbb{E}e^{\mathbf{u}^{\top}{\bm{\varphi}}_{0}}<A

and
\mathbb{E}e^{\mathbf{u}^{\top}{\bm{\varphi}}_{t}}\leq e^{B}+\mathbb{E}f({\bm{\varphi}}_{t})
, so this shows
that there exists some
C=C(A,\|\mathbf{u}\|,\mu)>0
for which

\mathbb{E}e^{\mathbf{u}^{\top}{\bm{\varphi}}_{t}}<C
and every
t\in[0,T]
.

Finally, for any
{\bm{\lambda}}\in\mathbb{R}^{p}
, we may write
{\bm{\lambda}}=\sum_{j=1}^{p}\mathbf{u}_{j}

where
\mathbf{u}_{1},\ldots,\mathbf{u}_{p}
are eigenvectors of
{\bm{\Gamma}}
. Under the given
finite moment-generating-function
condition for
q_{0}
, there exists some
A=A(q_{0},{\bm{\lambda}})>0
for which

\mathbb{E}_{{\bm{\varphi}}_{0}\sim q_{0}}e^{p\mathbf{u}_{j}^{\top}{\bm{\varphi}}_{0}}<A
for every
j=1,\ldots,p
.
Then for any
t\in[0,T]
, applying the above with
\mathbf{u}=p\mathbf{u}_{j}
yields

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}e^{{\bm{\lambda}}^{\top}{\bm{\varphi}}_{t}}=\mathbb{E}\prod_{j=1}^{p}e^{\mathbf{u}_{j}^{\top}{\bm{\varphi}}_{t}}\leq\prod_{j=1}^{p}\big(\mathbb{E}e^{p\mathbf{u}_{j}^{\top}{\bm{\varphi}}_{t}}\big)^{1/p}<C(q_{0},{\bm{\lambda}})

for some
C(q_{0},{\bm{\lambda}})<\infty
. This shows (
C.13
),
and specializing to
{\bm{\lambda}}\in\{\pm(8M/\tau^{2})\mathbf{e}_{j}\}_{j\in[p]}

for the standard basis vectors
\mathbf{e}_{j}
shows (
C.14
).
∎

Proof of Lemma
C.3
-
Part (3)
.

On a probability space containing
{\bm{\varphi}}_{0}\sim q_{0}
and a standard
Brownian motion
\{\mathbf{B}_{t}\}_{t\in[0,T]}
, define

{\bm{\varphi}}=\{{\bm{\varphi}}_{t}\}_{t\in[0,T]}
and

\widetilde{\bm{\varphi}}=\{\widetilde{{\bm{\varphi}}}_{t}\}_{t\in[0,T]}
as the (pathwise unique)
solutions to

\displaystyle{\bm{\varphi}}_{t}

\displaystyle={\bm{\varphi}}_{0}+\int_{0}^{t}-\nabla U_{g_{s}}({\bm{\varphi}}_{s})\mathrm{d}s+\sqrt{2}\mathbf{B}_{t},

\displaystyle\widetilde{{\bm{\varphi}}}_{t}

\displaystyle={\bm{\varphi}}_{0}+\int_{0}^{t}-\nabla U_{\widetilde{g}_{s}}(\widetilde{{\bm{\varphi}}}_{s})\mathrm{d}s+\sqrt{2}\mathbf{B}_{t}.

Thus
{\bm{\varphi}}\sim q
and
\widetilde{\bm{\varphi}}\sim\widetilde{q}
.
For any
t\in[0,T]
,

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\lVert{\bm{\varphi}}_{t}-\widetilde{{\bm{\varphi}}}_{t}\rVert^{2}

\displaystyle=\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\left\|\int_{0}^{t}\big(\nabla U_{g_{s}}({\bm{\varphi}}_{s})-\nabla U_{\widetilde{g}_{s}}(\widetilde{{\bm{\varphi}}}_{s})\big)\mathrm{d}s\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq T\cdot\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\int_{0}^{t}\bigg\lVert\nabla U_{g_{s}}({\bm{\varphi}}_{s})-\nabla U_{\widetilde{g}_{s}}(\widetilde{{\bm{\varphi}}}_{s})\bigg\rVert^{2}\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\leq 2T^{2}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\bigg\lVert\nabla U_{\widetilde{g}_{t}}({\bm{\varphi}}_{t})-\nabla U_{\widetilde{g}_{t}}(\widetilde{{\bm{\varphi}}}_{t})\bigg\rVert^{2}+2T\int_{0}^{T}\bigg\lVert\nabla U_{g_{t}}({\bm{\varphi}}_{t})-\nabla U_{\widetilde{g}_{t}}({\bm{\varphi}}_{t})\bigg\rVert^{2}\mathrm{d}t.

As shown in the proof of Part (1),

\nabla U_{g}:\mathbb{R}^{p}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{p}
is Lipschitz uniformly over
g\in\mathcal{P}(M)
,
so the first term is at most
CT^{2}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\lVert{\bm{\varphi}}_{t}-\widetilde{{\bm{\varphi}}}_{t}\rVert^{2}\leq\frac{1}{2}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\lVert{\bm{\varphi}}_{t}-\widetilde{{\bm{\varphi}}}_{t}\rVert^{2}
for some
C,T_{0}>0
and any
T\leq T_{0}
.
For the second term, using (
C.16
),

\displaystyle\lVert\nabla U_{g_{t}}({\bm{\varphi}}_{t})-\nabla U_{\widetilde{g}_{t}}({\bm{\varphi}}_{t})\rVert^{2}\leq C\sum_{j=1}^{p}\big(\theta_{g_{t}}(\varphi_{t,j})-\theta_{\widetilde{g}_{t}}(\varphi_{t,j})\big)^{2}.

Applying Proposition
B.1
, we have

\displaystyle\big|\theta_{g}(\varphi)-\theta_{\widetilde{g}}(\varphi)\big|

\displaystyle=\Big|\frac{\int_{-M}^{M}\theta\cdot\mathcal{N}_{\tau}(\varphi-\theta)g(\theta)\mathrm{d}\theta}{\int_{-M}^{M}\mathcal{N}_{\tau}(\varphi-\theta)g(\theta)\mathrm{d}\theta}-\frac{\int_{-M}^{M}\theta\cdot\mathcal{N}_{\tau}(\varphi-\theta)\widetilde{g}(\theta)\mathrm{d}\theta}{\int_{-M}^{M}\mathcal{N}_{\tau}(\varphi-\theta)\widetilde{g}(\theta)\mathrm{d}\theta}\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\int_{-M}^{M}\theta\cdot\mathcal{N}_{\tau}(\varphi-\theta)|g(\theta)-\widetilde{g}(\theta)|\mathrm{d}\theta}{\int_{-M}^{M}\mathcal{N}_{\tau}(\varphi-\theta)g(\theta)\mathrm{d}\theta}

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{-M}^{M}\theta\cdot\mathcal{N}_{\tau}(\varphi-\theta)\widetilde{g}(\theta)\mathrm{d}\theta\cdot\frac{\int_{-M}^{M}\mathcal{N}_{\tau}(\varphi-\theta)|g(\theta)-\widetilde{g}(\theta)|\mathrm{d}\theta}{\int_{-M}^{M}\mathcal{N}_{\tau}(\varphi-\theta)g(\theta)\mathrm{d}\theta\cdot\int_{-M}^{M}\mathcal{N}_{\tau}(\varphi-\theta)\widetilde{g}(\theta)\mathrm{d}\theta}

\displaystyle\leq Ce^{(4M/\tau^{2})|\varphi|}\cdot\int_{-M}^{M}|g(\theta)-\widetilde{g}(\theta)|\mathrm{d}\theta.

In summary, there exist
C^{\prime},T_{0}>0
such that for any
T\leq T_{0}
,

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\lVert{\bm{\varphi}}_{t}-\widetilde{{\bm{\varphi}}}_{t}\rVert^{2}\leq C^{\prime}T\int_{0}^{T}\sum_{j=1}^{p}e^{(4M/\tau^{2})|\varphi_{t,j}|}\mathrm{d}t\cdot d_{\infty,\mathrm{TV}}(g,\widetilde{g})^{2}.

Taking expectation on both sides and
using the bound (
C.14
) with
K(q_{0})=K
gives

d_{W}^{2}(q,\widetilde{q})\leq C_{0}^{2}T^{2}d_{\infty,\mathrm{TV}}(g,\widetilde{g})^{2}
,
for some
C_{0}>0
depending on
K
. This completes the proof.
∎

We now provide a lemma that will allow us to inductively establish the
smoothness of the preceding solutions
\{q_{t},g_{t}\}
in both space and time.

Lemma C.4
.

In the setting of Lemma
C.3
, suppose in addition that

g_{t}\in\mathcal{P}_{*}(M)
admits a density for each
t\in[0,T]
, where

t\mapsto g_{t}(\theta)
belongs to
C^{r}((0,T))
for some
r\geq 0
and every

\theta\in[-M,M]
,
and
\sup_{t\in(0,T)}\int_{-M}^{M}|\frac{\mathrm{d}^{a}}{\mathrm{d}t^{a}}g_{t}(\theta)|\mathrm{d}\theta<\infty
for all
a=0,\ldots,r
. Then:

1.

The map

t\mapsto f_{t}(\theta):=\mathcal{N}_{\tau}*\frac{\bar{q}_{t}}{\mathcal{N}_{\tau}*g_{t}}(\theta)
belongs
also to
C^{r}((0,T))
, and for all
a=0,\ldots,r
,

\sup_{t\in(0,T)}\sup_{\theta\in[-M,M]}\left|\frac{\mathrm{d}^{a}}{\mathrm{d}t^{a}}f_{t}(\theta)\right|<\infty.

(C.19)

2.

If
q_{0}\in\mathcal{P}_{*}(\mathbb{R}^{p})
has a continuous bounded density,
then the marginal laws
\{q_{t}\}_{t\in[0,T]}
of
\{{\bm{\varphi}}_{t}\}_{t\in[0,T]}

admit continuous densities bounded uniformly over
t\in[0,T]
. Furthermore if
r\geq 1
, then

(t,{\bm{\varphi}})\mapsto q_{t}({\bm{\varphi}})
belongs to
C^{r-1,\infty}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{p})
.

Proof.

For part (1), we claim by induction on
a\in\{0,1,\ldots,r\}

that
f_{t}(\theta)
belongs to

C^{a}((0,T))
, the bound (
C.19
) holds for
a
, and

\frac{\mathrm{d}^{a}}{\mathrm{d}t^{a}}f_{t}(\theta)=\mathbb{E}_{{\bm{\varphi}}\sim q_{t}}[F_{t,\theta}^{a}({\bm{\varphi}})]
for some
F_{t,\theta}^{a}({\bm{\varphi}})

that is a polynomial of the arguments

\varphi_{k},\qquad\frac{\int_{-M}^{M}\frac{\mathrm{d}^{i}}{\mathrm{d}\varphi_{k}^{i}}\mathcal{N}_{\tau}(\varphi_{k}-\theta)\frac{\mathrm{d}^{j}}{\mathrm{d}t^{j}}g_{t}(\theta)\mathrm{d}\theta}{\mathcal{N}_{\tau}*g_{t}(\varphi_{k})}\quad\text{ and }\quad\frac{\frac{\mathrm{d}^{i}}{\mathrm{d}\varphi_{k}^{i}}\mathcal{N}_{\tau}(\varphi_{k}-\theta)}{\mathcal{N}_{\tau}*g_{t}(\varphi_{k})}

(C.20)

for
k\in\{1,\ldots,p\}
,
i\geq 0
, and
j\in\{0,\ldots,a\}
.

For the base case
a=0
, observe that

f_{t}(\theta)=\mathcal{N}_{\tau}*\frac{\bar{q}_{t}}{\mathcal{N}_{\tau}*g_{t}}(\theta)=\mathbb{E}_{{\bm{\varphi}}\sim q_{t}}[F_{t,\theta}^{0}({\bm{\varphi}})],\qquad F_{t,\theta}^{0}({\bm{\varphi}}):=\frac{1}{p}\sum_{k=1}^{p}\frac{\mathcal{N}_{\tau}(\varphi_{k}-\theta)}{\mathcal{N}_{\tau}*g_{t}(\varphi_{k})}.

By the bound
\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\sup_{\theta\in[-M,M]}|\mathcal{N}_{\tau}(\varphi_{k}-\theta)/\mathcal{N}_{\tau}*g_{t}(\varphi_{k})|\leq Ce^{(2M/\tau^{2})|\varphi|}
from Proposition
B.1
and
Lemma
C.3
-(2),
we see that
\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\sup_{\theta\in[-M,M]}|f_{t}(\theta)|<\infty
, and the maps

{\bm{\varphi}}\mapsto F_{t,\theta}^{0}({\bm{\varphi}})q_{t}({\bm{\varphi}})
for
t\in[0,T]
are
uniformly integrable, hence
f_{t}(\theta)
is continuous in
t
. So the inductive
claims hold for
a=0
. Assuming they hold for
a-1
where
1\leq a\leq r
, we
have that

F_{t,\theta}^{a-1}({\bm{\varphi}})
belongs to
C^{1,\infty}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{p})
.
Then by Ito’s formula,

\frac{\mathrm{d}^{a}}{\mathrm{d}t^{a}}f_{t}(\theta)=\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\mathbb{E}[F_{t,\theta}^{a-1}({\bm{\varphi}})]=\mathbb{E}_{{\bm{\varphi}}\sim q_{t}}\left[\left(\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}F_{t,\theta}^{a-1}-\nabla U_{g_{t}}^{\top}\nabla F_{t,\theta}^{a-1}+\Delta F_{t,\theta}^{a-1}\right)({\bm{\varphi}})\right].

From the form (
C.15
), we have that each coordinate of

\nabla U_{g_{t}}({\bm{\varphi}})
is a polynomial of terms of the form
(
C.20
), and it is easily verified
that differentiating any term (
C.20
) in either
t
or
\varphi_{k}

yields again a polynomial of terms (
C.20
), where differentiating
in
t
increases the maximum order
j
of the time derivative of
g_{t}(\theta)

by at most 1. Thus the above may be written as

\frac{\mathrm{d}^{a}}{\mathrm{d}t^{a}}f_{t}(\theta)=\mathbb{E}_{{\bm{\varphi}}\sim q_{t}}[F_{t,\theta}^{a}({\bm{\varphi}})]
where
F_{t,\theta}^{a}({\bm{\varphi}})
is a
polynomial of the terms (
C.20
) with
j\leq a
.
By Proposition
B.1
, for each fixed
i\geq 0
we have

\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\sup_{\theta\in[-M,M]}|\frac{\mathrm{d}^{i}}{\mathrm{d}\varphi_{k}^{i}}\mathcal{N}_{\tau}(\varphi_{k}-\theta)/\mathcal{N}*g_{t}(\varphi_{k})|\leq Ce^{C|\varphi_{k}|}
for some
C>0
.
Then, applying the given condition
\sup_{t\in(0,T)}\int_{-M}^{M}|\frac{\mathrm{d}^{a}}{\mathrm{d}t^{a}}g_{t}(\theta)|\mathrm{d}\theta<\infty
and
Lemma
C.3
-(2),
we get
\sup_{t\in(0,T)}\sup_{\theta\in[-M,M]}|\frac{\mathrm{d}^{a}}{\mathrm{d}t^{a}}f_{t}(\theta)|<\infty
. Furthermore,

{\bm{\varphi}}\mapsto F_{t,\theta}^{a}({\bm{\varphi}})q_{t}({\bm{\varphi}})
is uniformly
integrable over
t\in(0,T)
,
so
\frac{\mathrm{d}^{a}}{\mathrm{d}t^{a}}f_{t}(\theta)
is continuous
in
t
. This completes the induction, showing the inductive claim
for all
a=0,\ldots,r
, and hence establishes Part (1).

For Part (2), let us write
q_{t}({\bm{\varphi}})\in H^{j,k}\equiv H^{j,k}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{p})
if all components of the weak derivatives
D_{t}^{a}D_{\bm{\varphi}}^{b}q_{t}({\bm{\varphi}})
for
a\leq j
and
b\leq k
exist and belong to
L^{r}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{p})
(defined with respect to Lebesgue measure) for every
r<\infty
.
We write
q_{t}({\bm{\varphi}})\in H_{\text{loc}}^{j,k}
if for any compact subset
S\subset(0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{p}
, these derivatives belong to
L^{r}(S)
for every

r<\infty
. By Holder’s inequality and the Leibniz rule for weak
differentiation,
H_{\text{loc}}^{j,k}
is closed under sums and products.
Lemma
C.3
-(2) implies that for any fixed

k\geq 0
, some
C,C^{\prime}>0
, and all
t\in[0,T]
,

\mathbb{E}_{{\bm{\varphi}}\sim q_{t}}[\|{\bm{\varphi}}\|_{2}^{k}]\leq C\sum_{j=1}^{p}\mathbb{E}_{{\bm{\varphi}}\sim q_{t}}[|\varphi_{j}|^{k}]\leq C^{\prime}.

(C.21)

Then the expression (
C.16
) for
\nabla U_{g_{t}}

implies, for any fixed
k>0
, that

\mathbb{E}_{{\bm{\varphi}}\sim q_{t}}[\|\nabla U_{g_{t}}({\bm{\varphi}})\|^{k}]<C
uniformly over
t\in[0,T]
.
Then
[
BKRS22
, Corollaries 6.4.3, 7.3.8]
show that

\{q_{t}\}_{t\in[0,T]}
admit continuous densities with respect to Lebesgue
measure, uniformly bounded over
t\in[0,T]
, which further satisfy

q_{t}({\bm{\varphi}})\in H_{\text{loc}}^{0,1}.

(C.22)

To improve this smoothness to
H_{\text{loc}}^{r,\infty}
, we
apply a similar argument as in
[
JKO98
, Theorem 5.1]
:
Fix
(t,{\bm{\varphi}})\in(0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{p}
and apply the Ito formula
(
C.17
) with

\zeta_{s}(\mathbf{x})=\omega(s)\eta(\mathbf{x})\mathcal{N}_{2(\delta+t-s)}({\bm{\varphi}}-\mathbf{x})
,
where
\delta>0
,
\eta:\mathbb{R}^{p}\to[0,1]
is a smooth compactly supported
function on
\mathbb{R}^{p}
, and
\omega:\mathbb{R}\to[0,1]
is a smooth compactly supported
function satisfying
\omega(s)=0
for all
s\leq 0
and
\omega(t)=1
.
Then, applying
\partial_{s}\mathcal{N}_{2(\delta+t-s)}({\bm{\varphi}}-\mathbf{x})+\Delta\mathcal{N}_{2(\delta+t-s)}({\bm{\varphi}}-\mathbf{x})=0
(the heat equation) and the chain
rule, (
C.17
) yields

\displaystyle\int\eta(\mathbf{x})q_{t}(\mathbf{x})\mathcal{N}_{2\delta}({\bm{\varphi}}-\mathbf{x})\mathrm{d}\mathbf{x}

\displaystyle=\int_{-\infty}^{t}\int\Big[\omega^{\prime}(s)\eta(\mathbf{x})-\omega(s)\nabla U_{g_{s}}(\mathbf{x})^{\top}\nabla\eta(\mathbf{x})+\omega(s)\Delta\eta(\mathbf{x})\Big]\mathcal{N}_{2(\delta+t-s)}({\bm{\varphi}}-\mathbf{x})q_{s}(\mathbf{x})\mathrm{d}\mathbf{x}\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\hskip 72.26999pt+\int_{-\infty}^{t}\int\Big[\omega(s)\eta(\mathbf{x})\nabla U_{g_{s}}(\mathbf{x})-\omega(s)\nabla\eta(\mathbf{x})\Big]^{\top}\nabla\mathcal{N}_{2(\delta+t-s)}({\bm{\varphi}}-\mathbf{x})q_{s}(\mathbf{x})\mathrm{d}\mathbf{x}\mathrm{d}s.

Applying the weak differentiability in
\mathbf{x}
of
q_{s}(\mathbf{x})

from (
C.22
)
to integrate by parts in the second term, and taking the limit
\delta\to 0
,
this yields

\displaystyle\eta({\bm{\varphi}})q_{t}({\bm{\varphi}})

\displaystyle=\int_{-\infty}^{t}\Big[\Big(q_{s}[\omega^{\prime}(s)\eta-\omega(s)\nabla U_{g_{s}}^{\top}\nabla\eta+\omega(s)\Delta\eta]

\displaystyle\hskip 72.26999pt-\nabla\cdot(q_{s}[\omega(s)\eta\nabla U_{g_{s}}-\omega(s)\nabla\eta])\Big)*\mathcal{N}_{t-s}\Big]({\bm{\varphi}})\mathrm{d}s.

(C.23)

The estimate of

[
LSU68
, Chapter 4, (3.1)]
shows the implication

f_{t}({\bm{\varphi}})\in H^{j,k}\text{ for all }2j+k\leq 2m\Rightarrow\int_{-\infty}^{t}[f_{s}*\mathcal{N}_{t-s}]({\bm{\varphi}})\mathrm{d}s\in H^{j,k}\text{ for all }2j+k\leq 2m+2.

(C.24)

From (
C.15
) and the given condition
